1 Introduction
Workplace collaboration is vital to an organization’s success. Through collaboration,
organizations can innovate faster, make decisions faster, grow their business successfully, and
remain competitive. It is also abundantly clear that organizations need to adapt to the way
their employees work so the organization can collaborate and compete more effectively.
This guide introduces you to the Deltapath UC platform, which
• allows you to manage fewer pieces of equipment and applications because of its all-in-
one solution architecture.
• allows all Polycom® endpoints to connect and register to Deltapath UC, a central
controller and a partner solution for Polycom video endpoint systems such as
RealPresence® Trio, HDX series (4000, 6000-9000), RealPresence® Group series
(300/500/700), RealPresence® Mobile (RPM), RealPresence® Desktop (RPD),
RealPresence® Collaboration Server (RMX), and VVX® Business Media phones.
• interoperates with numerous brands of devices and supports auto-provisioning for most
of these brands. All information can be found in Appendix 2.
• empowers mobile conferencing and mobile collaboration anywhere at anytime
• integrates with Salesforce, the number one Customer Relationship Management (CRM)
provider that enables organizations to match incoming calls with existing customer
data so they can provide superior service by resolving customer issues faster,
identifying and closing sales opportunities faster, and staying on top of sales tasks.
• embraces Instant Message (IM) in the workplace, which supports a different form of
communication. IM is especially useful where real-time communication is needed, but
not necessarily spoken.
• allows organizations to host audio conference calls for free with a built-in conference
bridge
• allows integration between Polycom and third-party PBX
• helps users share content wirelessly through RealPresence® Mobile and RealPresence®
Desktop
• offers flexible deployment options that fits any organization’s strategies
• allows you to integrate with Skype for Business which means another communication
channel for organizations.
• allows organizations to distribute phone calls from a single telephone number to a
group of designated people at different numbers that belong to a Hunt Group, in an
effort to assist customer needs both effectively and efficiently. The Hunt Group offers
3 different ring strategies to meet the needs of different organizations.
About this Guide
The Deltapath UC Platform guide, release 5.3, is a major release consisting of many library
updates to enhance functionality and security. Please note, there is no upgrade path for the
Switchboard application on either PC or macOS. After upgrading to UC 5.3, you must uninstall
the old Switchboard application before downloading and installing the new switchboard
application, release 5.3.
The guide is aimed at familiarizing administrators with the features and functionalities of the
Deltapath UC Platform and bringing attention to important design considerations. Next,

2 Installing the Deltapath UC platform
This section provides information on setting up your hardware.
2.1 Package Contents
The package contents are listed below for three Deltapath UC platforms, U32, U256, and
U512.
• 1x Deltapath UC platform
• 10 x Screws for U32, U256, and U512 models
• 2x Mounting Brackets
• 1x Power Cord for U32 and 2 for U256 and U512 models
2.2 Positioning the Deltapath UC platform
Deltapath UC platforms are designed to be rack mounted. To disassemble or reassemble the
mounting brackets, a Philips screwdriver is required.
Always disconnect the power cord before conducting any installation.
Assembling and Disassembling the Mounting Brackets
All Deltapath UC servers follow the same steps for assembling and dissembling mounting
brackets.
To disassemble the brackets, follow the information below:
1. Unplug unit from its power source.
2. Use a Phillips screwdriver to remove the screws that are securing the brackets to the
Deltapath UC platform.
To reassemble the brackets, follow the instructions below:
1. Unplug unit from its power source.
2. Attach the 2 L-shape mounting brackets to the sides of the Deltapath UC servers using the
screws provided and a Phillips screwdriver.
Rack Mounting a Unit
All physical requirements for rack mounting the different Deltapath UC servers are listed in
the table below:

3 Virtual Machine
If you are running a Deltapath VM Edition, you have the option to purchase a USB dongle,
otherwise known as your license key. In the event you want to migrate your Deltapath UC VM
from server x to server y, you will not need to request a new license from Deltapath because
the original license will not recognize the hardware on the new server. If you used a dongle
with server x, you can reuse it in server y. Although optional, the USB dongle can make the
migration process simpler because you do not have to request a new license.
4 Power Management of the Deltapath UC Platform
There are many options for managing the power to the Deltapath UC platform. Power can be
managed locally or remotely. Specifically, you can manage power directly from the platform,
the command line interface, and the administrator UI from a browser.
1. Reboot/Reset Button: A reboot button is located on the front panel of the Deltapath UC
platform. Reboot the system without unplugging the power by inserting a small item such
as the point of a paperclip into the open slot.
2. Power on and Off Button: The power button is in the back of the unit. After you initiate a
shutdown from the administrator UI or the Command Line Interface, which is discussed
directly below, you can use the power button to turn on the unit. The power cord must
still be connected to use the power button. The power button can also be used to turn off
the unit.
Note: When using the button at the back of a unit to power it down, press and hold the
button for 3-5 seconds. Since there is a safety circuit built-in, if you unplug the power,
wait 5 seconds before connecting the power again.
3. Auto-Recovery: If an improper shutdown occurred due to an event such as an outage or
the power cord was unplugged and later plugged in again, the auto-power initiates and
turns on the Deltapath UC platform once the unit detects power. Unless the unit is
powered down by the administrator through the administrator UI or directly from the
console, the Deltapath UC platform switch is always in the ON position.

6 Planning for Your Installation
Planning is important before any installation in order to reach a desired outcome. This section
introduces network and cluster design considerations and prepares you to make decisions
about classifying and prioritizing packets. Specifically, this section helps you decide how the
Deltapath UC
unit will connect to your network and what type of clustering, if any, will be used to add high
availability and disaster recovery.
Cluster Design Consideration
Two standard cluster designs are discussed, local cluster and geographical cluster. Both a
local cluster and geographical cluster involve a group of independent Deltapath UC units.
These units work together to increase the availability of the applications and services
associated with Deltapath UC. If one of the units fail, the failover process initiates. Another
unit in the group begins to seamlessly provide service. As a result, users experience minimum
disruption to service.
6.1 High Availability and Local Cluster
Some devices and SIP peer only allow connection to one IP address, which is typically the case
when you have a SIP trunk. In this situation, use local cluster with IP takeover functionality.
In the event the primary unit fails, the secondary unit will automatically come online with the
same IP address as the primary. When the same IP address is used, users experience minimal
disruption to service because there is no change to the IP address.

-- 25 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 25
Figure 1: High Availability and Local Cluster
The Deltapath UC unit has six Ethernet ports. The SIP trunk connects to Uplink-1. A straight
ethernet cable is connected between Port 2 on the primary unit and Port 2 on the secondary
unit. A straight ethernet cable connects HA-1 on the primary unit to HA-1 on the secondary
unit. If a unit is turned off or fails, Uplink-1 will route all signals to HA-1 and then to the LAN
switch. An additional ethernet cable connects HA-2 on the primary unit with HA-2 on the
secondary unit. Uplink-2 is connected to the LAN Switch. The local cluster and the PBX also
connect to the LAN switch.
As a best practice, the primary and secondary Deltapath UC units should be
configured with IP Takeover mode. In the event the primary unit fails; the
secondary unit will automatically come online with the same IP address as the
primary. When the same IP address is used, users experience minimal
disruption to service because there is no change to the IP address. In the event
he primary and secondary do not share the same IP address, users will either
need to rely on DNS to redirect to the secondary or wait for the connection to
the primary to timeout before routing to the secondary.
Some devices and SIP peer only allow connection to one IP address such as a SIP trunk. In
addition, single-site failover can take up to 3 minutes for phones to connect to the backup
server. When the primary server resumes, it can also take up to 3 minutes for full recovery.
An IP takeover cluster within a single physical site provides redundancy if the primary
Deltapath UC platform hardware or software fails. This design does not provide protection in
the event the physical site becomes unavailable in the event of a disaster or power outage.
See the illustration below for more information of a failover cluster in a single physical site.

-- 26 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 26
Figure 2: High Availability Environment with a Gateway
Devices register to the primary Deltapath UC platform and the secondary Deltapath UC
platform. The gateway will forward calls to the primary Deltapath UC platform for further
processing. In the event the primary Deltapath UC platform becomes unavailable; the
gateway detects the issues and redirects all calls to the secondary Deltapath UC platform for
processing. For additional redundancy and disaster recovery, a multi-site failover cluster
spanning two different locations such as San Jose and Hong Kong can be implemented.
High Availability in a Geographical Cluster
In this design, the Deltapath UC platforms are placed in two different physical locations to
ensure high availability and disaster recovery. This design consideration provides redundancy
in the event of a disaster in the primary datacenter. To ensure successful failover in this
design, an administrator must create the DNS SRV records associated with the configuration
since each site does not share the same IP address. See DNS SRV for more information.

-- 27 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 27
Figure 3: Multi-Site Failover Cluster with Two Deltapath UC platforms
Note, when the primary server is down, all calls will still route to the primary server before
routing to the backup server. As a result, there will be a delay to service when
making/receiving calls.
Lastly, if Force RTP is enabled on the server, all RTP Packets will pass through the server
irrelevant of whether you configure a multi-site failover cluster or a single site failover
cluster. When the primary server fails, any user in an active conversation will be
disconnected.
By default, Deltapath will advertise both the primary and secondary server IP to the following
Polycom endpoints: Polycom VVX, SoundPoint IP/SoundStation IP, and RealPresence Trio.
Geographical Failover Behavior and Endpoints
The following devices have a known limitation in geographical cluster mode.
Brand Endpoint Models
Cisco 6921 78XX
series
7912 7931 7942 7962 8XXX
series
99XX
series
AudioCodes All series
In this section, learn about the expected behavior of different endpoints during a failover so
you can restore functionality fast and without surprises.

-- 28 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 28
Know Limitation & Workaround:
During failover mode, the above endpoints will register to the backup server and operate as
usual. However, it cannot roll back to the primary server automatically when the primary
server resumes service. Instead, you need to execute a reboot of the concerned endpoints
for them to roll back to the primary server. Therefore, if you have a mix environment of
endpoints, such as Polycom and the concerned Cisco phones, it is recommended to use local
cluster, if possible, to avoid Polycom users registering on the primary while Cisco users
registering on the backup with the two groups not being able to call to each other. If
geographical cluster is mandatory, we recommend to only reinstate the primary server at a
scheduled downtime and arrange a reboot of all concerned phones shortly after.
Deltapath’s engineering team created a geographical cluster test environment to conduct
tests on a secondary server after the primary server became unreachable or was intentionally
taken offline. The results are recorded below.
Scenarios and Results
All tests were conducted on the secondary server once the primary server became
unreachable.
6.1.2.1 Registration:
➢ Registration behavior when primary server becomes unreachable (unplugged/network
issues)
✓ Polycom and Cisco endpoints register as expected
➢ Registration behavior when primary server encounters an internal failure not related
to network connectivity
✓ Polycom and Cisco endpoints register as expected
6.1.2.2 Inbound calls:
➢ Primary is unreachable due to network issues
✓ All tested Polycom and Cisco endpoints are functional
Note: All inbound calls experience a 10-15 second delay.
➢ Primary is unreachable due to non-network related issues
✓ All tested Polycom and Cisco endpoints are functional
Note: Cisco endpoints may experience a 10-15 second delay.
6.1.2.3 Outbound calls:
➢ Primary is unreachable due to network issues
✓ All tested Polycom and Cisco endpoints are functional
Note: All outbound calls experience a 10-15 second delay.
➢ Primary is unreachable due to non-network related issues
✓ All tested Polycom and Cisco endpoints are functional
Note: Cisco endpoints may experience a 10-15 second delay.

6.1.2.4 Rebooted Endpoints in failover:
The following phones have known issues: (See details below)
• Cisco 7911
• Cisco 7941
• Cisco 7940
• Cisco 7960
Note: Rebooted phones may take 3-5 minutes to re-register and will display incorrect dates
and times.
Cisco 7911:
This model will experience the following issues if rebooted when a geographical failover takes
place.
1. The endpoint will continuously display “Registering.”
2. The endpoint will ring when called, but users will be unable to answer.
3. Users will not hear a dial tone when lifting the receiver when the phone is in an idle state.
4. Users will not be able to place or receive calls.
Note: Once access to the primary is restored, the 7911 will take 2-3 minutes to become fully
functional. During this time, users may experience one-way audio issues.
Cisco 7941:
This model will experience the following issues if rebooted when a geographical failover takes
place.
1. The endpoint will continuously display “Registering.”
2. The endpoint will ring when called, but users will be unable to answer.
3. Users will not hear a dial tone when lifting the receiver when the phone is in an idle state.
4. Users will not be able to place or receive calls.
Cisco 7940 & 7960:
These models will experience the following issues if rebooted during a failover scenario:
1. The endpoints will display a “Line Unregistered” message, but users will still be able to
make and receive calls.
6.1.2.5 Adding, Removing, or Changing a Phonebook Record Directly on an Endpoint
The following will occur if a user adds, removes, or changes a phonebook record directly on
an endpoint during failover:
• Changes will not be synced to the server
• Entries will not be displayed on Switchboard
• If the endpoint is rebooted, new entries will still be visible
For Cisco models 7940, 7960, 7911, 7912, and 7961:
• Endpoints are unable to connect to the External Directory

-- 30 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 30
• Endpoints are unable to view Phonebook Lists
6.1.2.6 Adding, Removing, or Changing a Phonebook Record on the Backup Server
The following will occur if a user adds, removes, or changes a phonebook record on the
backup server while the primary is offline or unreachable:
• All endpoints will not be updated to reflect changes made on the backup server
• When the primary becomes available, all phonebook changes made on the backup
server will be lost.
For Cisco models 7940, 7960, 7911, 7912, and 7961:
• Endpoints are unable to connect to the External Directory
• Endpoints are unable to view Phonebook Lists
6.1.2.7 Shared Line Appearance
Any endpoints using SLA will cease to function during a failover situation. In order to restore
functionality, administrators must disable SLA on all effected endpoints and perform a
reboot. There is a workaround, however.
SLA Workaround
To maintain SLA functionality during a failover situation when using a geographical cluster,
administrators can configure an SRV record and a single A record on their DNS server. The SRV
record must also be configured on the Deltapath UC server. The A record must be pointed to
the primary Deltapath server during normal operation and will need to be updated to point to
the backup Deltapath server during a failover.
The administrator must also change the Device Settings under User Settings for all
Cisco endpoints to TCP. Without changing this setting, all Cisco endpoints utilizing UDP
will un-register within 24 hours.

-- 31 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 31
6.1.2.8 DHCP
Deltapath does not recommend using the Deltapath UC platform as a DHCP server when a
geographical cluster is used. Phones will be unable to register after their DHCP lease expires
if the primary server is unreachable. Deltapath suggests using a third party DHCP server for
this type of configuration.
Single Deltapath UC platform without a Cluster
If you decide a clustering solution is not required, then you can operate one Deltapath UC
platform. Connect the RJ45 cable to the port you configured your network IP address. In this
case, you may use any network port. For example, a company may have a single Deltapath
UC platform and a PSTN Gateway.
Figure 4: Single Deltapath UC platform with a Gateway

-- 32 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 32
All devices register to the Deltapath UC platform. Calls coming in from the PSTN go to the
gateway, which forwards the calls to the Deltapath UC platform. The Deltapath UC platform
then forwards the incoming call to the correct extension. If there is a network outage that
prevents the phones from connecting to the Deltapath UC platform, you will experience a
disruption in service.
6.2 Network Design Considerations
Two network designs are discussed, Typical and Alternative. In a Typical network design, the
Deltapath UC platform acts as a firewall with one of its interfaces connecting directly to the
WAN and the other interface connecting to the internal LAN IP. In the Alternative network
design, the Deltapath UC platform sits inside your LAN. Port forward a WAN IP with one-to-
one NAT mapping to the internal IP of the Deltapath UC platform.

In this network design, the Deltapath UC platform is placed on the DMZ behind a corporate
firewall that manages the WAN. The network topology is slightly more complex in this
configuration, but necessary in some organizations due to the organization’s security policies.

6.4 Prepare Network for Quality of Service (QoS)
It is worthwhile taking the time to classify your IP packets before actual setup. Use Quality of
Service prioritize network traffic through IP packet prioritization.
Figure 7: Bandwidth without IP Packet Prioritization Applied
Figure 8: Bandwidth with IP Packet Prioritization Applied
Consider the type of prioritization you want to assign the packets below:
• Type of Service (TOS) for SIP packets
• TOS for RTP audio packets
• TOS for RTP video packets
• TOS for RTP text packets
• 802.1p priority for SIP packets
• 802.1p priority for RTP audio packets
• 802.1p priority for RTP video packets
Bandwidth with Quality of Service Rules Applied

-- 36 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 36
With QoS rules in place, your network devices know what packets to give priority to in order
to ensure bandwidth availability.
Administrators can configure their own priorities based on traffic type and other network
parameters.
Observe the different classification structures below. The DiffServ model has two types of
forwarding classes: AF and EF. Expedited forwarding (EF) is given strict priority queuing above
all other traffic classes. Assured Forwarding (AF) is provided as long as the subscribed rate is
not exceeded. If traffic exceeds the rate prescribed, assurance of delivery can be
compromised.
Class 1 Class 2 Class 3 Class 4
Low Drop
Probability
AF11 (DSCP 10) AF21 (DSCP 18) AF31 (DSCP 26) AF41 (DSCP 34)
Medium
Drop
Probability
AF12 (DSCP 12) AF22 (DSCP 20) AF32 (DSCP 28) AF42 (DSCP 36)
High Drop
Probability
AF13 (DSCP 14) AF23 (DSCP 22) AF33 (DSCP 30) AF43 (DSCP 38)
Table 4: Assured Forwarding (AF) Behavior Groups for Classifying and Managing Network Traffic
DSCP Binary Hex Decimal Typical Application Examples
CS0
(Default)
000 000 0x00 0
CS1 001 000 0x08 8 Scavenger YouTube, Gaming, P2P
CS2 010 000 0x10 16 OAM SNMP, SSH, Syslog
CS3 011 000 0x18 24 Signaling SIP, H.323
CS4 100 000 0x20 32 Realtime Video and Voice
CS5 101 000 0x28 40 Broadcast video Cisco IPVS
CS6 110 000 0x30 48 Network control EIFRP, OSPF, HSRP, IKE
CS7 111 000 0x38 56
Table 5: Class Selector Values
Configure your network equipment to give higher priority to the voice VLAN
traffic to guarantee bandwidth for voice packets across your network. You may

-- 37 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 37
also choose to give higher priority to voice packets over video packets as audio
is crucial in every meeting. Many QoS parameters can be configured for Polycom
phone RTP packets and other Polycom endpoints.

7 Quick Start
In most cases, you will need to configure all the essential parameters in the sequence listed
below before the system is fully operational. Planning ahead is recommended to ensure
efficiency and success. For example, collect user requirements in advance as the configuration
approach may differ depending on the different groups of users.
1. Plan ahead. Decide how Deltapath UC platform will connect to your network, what type of
clustering, if any, will be used, and decide if it is necessary to prioritize IP packets using
QoS.
2. Configure network interfaces based on your network design, Typical or Alternative. Use
Command Line Interface either through SSH or Console port.
3. Change default administrator account credentials.
4. If you are using two Deltapath UC platforms, configure a local cluster for a single-site
failover cluster or a multi-site failover cluster for high availability and disaster recovery.
5. Activate built-in firewall on untrusted interface.
6. Customize enterprise specific settings under Configuration / General Settings such as User
Profile, Contexts, E-mail Templates, and Permission Groups.
7. Setup and provision gateway to connect to the PSTN if you have purchased one.
8. Configure DNS.

-- 38 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 38
9. Procure SSL Certificate and upload the CRT file.
10. Create the necessary Inbound and outbound routing.
11. Create Users.
12. Add SIP Trunk and outbound routing to other Deltapath UC platform and/or any third-
party SIP based PBX if needed.
13. If you have a VVX D60, pair the VVX D60 base station with a VVX Business Media phone.
Register the wireless handset with the base station.
14. Setup Corporate directory if you have an LDAP or Microsoft Active Directory in service.

Real Time Call Quality
Whenever employees report they or their customers are having call quality issues (noise,
voice distortion, audio delay, etc.), you can verify the call quality of the active call. The real-
time call quality charts capture data that can be used to analyze, diagnose poor call quality,
and eventually remedy the issue. The Real Time Call Quality data refresh rate is every 10
seconds. You can also conduct a historical review of call quality in CDR.
When users report they are having poor call quality, open the Real Time Call Quality tool.
1. Enter the number of the user or click the dropdown arrow and then click on a user.
2. Click Start.

-- 50 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 50
Mean opinion score (MOS) is commonly used to measure video, audio, and audiovisual quality
evaluation, but it is not restricted to these modalities. MOS is typically expressed as a single
rational number, typically in the range 1-5, where 1 is lowest perceived quality, and 5 is the
highest perceived quality. The MOS of a call will only be available on devices that support it
like Deltapath Mobile and Polycom/Poly endpoints.
When troubleshooting call quality, it is important to know that Deltapath UC uses jitters,
packet loss, and latency to calculate the mean opinion score. Therefore, when you have a
poor rating, it will be attributed to one or more of these things. Use performance diagnostic
tools like Wireshark to analyze packets to diagnose your call quality issues.

7.6 Audit Trail
Audit logs help companies ensure protocols are followed and monitor and track potential
security breaches or internal misuses of information. The audit trail log documents user
events in Deltapath’s switchboard. The audit trail includes a date and time stamp, username,
IP address, what resources were accessed, and what actions were taken. For example, when
users download or playback call recordings, these actions are logged.
Export Audit Trail logs by clicking Export (Filtered) or Export (All) at the bottom of the
screen.
7.7 Log Management
From the Web UI, you can manage logs by downloading or deleting them for Audit Trails, Call
Services, Dynamic Filtering, and FTP to free up disk space. Log Management is found under
Audit Trail.

8.3 Connecting to Deltapath UC platform for the First Time
Before you can place the Deltapath UC platform in service, it needs to connect to your network
via Console or Ethernet Access. This section describes the first step in configuring the Deltapath
UC platform, which is IP address assignment. Two methods are introduced, console port access
and SSH access.
Console Access
You will require a console cable and a USB to Serial adapter to connect to your computer.
Follow the steps below.
1. Launch the Putty application.
2. Connection Type: Select Serial.
3. Serial line: Enter the Com Port your console cable is connected to.
Use your Windows Device Manager to discover what Com Port you are using.
4. Click Serial located under the Category window and configure Putty based on the settings
below:
• Bps/Data Bits/Parity/Stop Bits: 38400 8N1

-- 55 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 55
• Hardware Flow Control: No
• Software Flow Control: No
5. Click Open and login with your administrator credentials.
Proceed to step 6, which can be found in the section titled, Assigning an IP address to
Deltapath UC platform from CLI.
Ethernet Access
If you do not have a console cable or USB to Serial adapter, connect your PC network port to
the Ethernet port of the Deltapath UC.
1. Connect the Ethernet cable directly between your computer and Port 2 of the Deltapath
UC and then enter the complete IP address settings.
2. Assign your computer the following IP address, 169.254.0.4.
3. Assign Subnet Mask: 255.255.255.0
4. Use Putty in SSH mode to connect to the factory default IP, 169.254.0.1.
5. Login with your administrator’s credentials.
Proceed to step 6 below under Assigning an IP address to Deltapath UC platform from CLI.
Whether you are connecting with a console cable or and Ethernet cable, the command lines are
the same. Continue to follow the steps below.
Assigning an IP Address to Deltapath UC platform from CLI

1. Type password on the command line after you log in with your default administrator
credentials.
2. Please enter your current password: Example: P2x13a
3. Please enter your new password: Example: DeltaP17
4. Please enter it again: Example: DeltaP17
Passwords can include most symbols except for a space, backslash, slash, single
quote, double quote, and grave accent.

Using Personal Schedules
Users can set schedules for their personal phones that reflect their own personal work
schedule. Schedules contain rules and policies that dictate what happens to calls when a
particular schedule is activated or deactivated. A personal schedule can be operated in
Simple Mode or Advanced mode.
Simple Mode
Users who want to change their schedules on an on-demand basis should use Simple Mode. In
Simple Mode, there is no timeslot in place, which automates schedules. Instead, users toggle
to the schedule that fits them at any time. For example, a user can have a schedule (number
status) for Office Hour which will ring the desk phone of the user, and an After-Office Hours
schedule (number status), which will ring the user’s mobile number. The user may set his
extension to Office Hour mode for as long as he likes and then change it to After-Office Hour
mode. This is convenient if you have a flexible work schedule.
Advanced Mode
Users often create multiple schedules to help them work more effectively and efficiently. In
Advanced mode, more than one schedule can run because each number status is connected to
a timeslot. See the example below:
➢ Irene creates a Daytime Work schedule for the phone to ring between 8am – 5:00pm.
➢ Irene Also creates a Meeting schedule that runs between 2pm – 4pm each day.

-- 76 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 76
➢ Lastly, create an After-Hour schedule that operates between 5:01pm - 7:59am where
all calls are sent to voicemail.
In Advanced mode, all schedules are automatically activated. This way, users do not have to
remember to deactivate or activate a schedule, which gets very difficult when multiple
schedules exist. For you to assist users who are attempting to create schedules, read the
steps below.
Currently, Peter Petrosi only has the schedule Ring30_VM activated – as illustrated in the
example below.

PC Presence Threshold Settings: By default, PC Presence Threshold Settings, Away and

What does Away and Extended Away mean?
A user’s PC can be set to away or to extended away after a predefined amount of time.
➢ Away: User is connected to a network and logged into the switchboard. However, the user
is temporarily away from the computer. The user’s PC Presence is displayed as a yellow
icon as seen below in the screenshot.
➢ Extended Away: when User has been inactive on the switchboard for an extended amount
of time. The user’s PC Presence is displayed as a red icon.
Note: When the green icon is displayed, the user is connected to a network, logged into the
switchboard. Offline means the user is not connected to a network and logged out of the
switchboard.

1. Select Customize to change the PC Presence time.

3. Switch to Extended Away after: Enter a number.

5. Click OK.

10.4 DNS A Record and DNS SRV Record
Add associated DNS A and SRV records in the following situations.
DNS A Record
Use a DNS A record to map a fully qualified domain name (FQDN) to an IP address. For
example, an A record is used to point a domain name such as server1.ktech.com to the IP
address of Ktech’s hosting server, 74.125.225.147.
If you have a backup server, you should create another FQDN such as server2.ktech.com and
select Secondary under Record Type to ensure the endpoints configuration on the backup
server is also using the FQDN and not the IP address.
Follow the steps below to set up a DNS A record.
1. Click
2. Type DNS Record in the textbox.
3. Click DNS Record. The DNS Record dialog box opens.

-- 98 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 98
4. Click Add DNS Record.
5. Network Interface: Click the dropdown and then Click an IP address.
6. Domain Name: Enter a domain name.
7. Record Type: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click A Record.
8. Server Type: Primary.
9. Click OK.
10. Reboot phones. See Equipment.
DNS SRV
It is important to have the SRV record defined and point to the A record for both the primary
and secondary servers with the desired priority and weight.
Ensure the DNS SRV records that need to be defined on the DNS Server are completed to
ensure you achieve geographical cluster redundancy. All DNS SRV records follow the form
defined in the table below.
_service._proto.name. TTL class SRV priority weight port target.
• service: Identifies the name of the service. Example: SIP
• proto: Identifies the transport protocol of the service such as TCP or UDP
• name.: Identifies the domain name of the record Example:
Deltapath.deltapath.com.
• TTL: Identifies the lifespan of the SRV record Example: 86400 seconds = 1 day
• class: Identifies the standard DNS class field, which is always IN
• priority: Identifies the priority of the target host. Lower values have higher priority
• weight: A relative weight is defined for records with the same priority. The higher
the value, the higher the priority
• port: Identifies the TCP or UDP port the service is located on
• target.: Identifies the hostname of the matching providing the service
Table 17: Parts and Definition of a DNS SRV Record

-- 99 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 99
For a standard Deltapath UC deployment you would need the following DNS entries regardless
of whether this is a standalone, local cluster, or geographical cluster.
_cmaconfig._tcp.deltapath.com. 120 IN SRV 10 100 443 primary.deltapath.com.
The line above enables users to find the available Deltapath UC so they can login from their
RealPresence Mobile or RealPresence Desktop with their e-mail address. In this example, the
entry is created in the Deltapath.com domain; therefore, if the user enters an e-mail address
@deltapath.com, the Deltapath UC will automatically be discovered, preventing the
administrator from having to introduce a Server IP or hostname to the users.
Optional:
_sip._tcp.uc.deltapath.com
86400 IN SRV 10 100 5060
_sip._udp.uc.deltapath.com 86400 IN SRV 20 100 5060
The next section shows a DNS SRV configuration for Deltapath.com domain.
_sip._tcp.Deltapath.deltapath.com. 86400 IN SRV 10 100 5060 primary.deltapath.com.
_sip._tcp.Deltapath.deltapath.com. 86400 IN SRV 20 100 5060 backup.deltapath.com.
_sip._udp.Deltapath.deltapath.com. 86400 IN SRV 10 100 5060 primary.deltapath.com.
_sip._udp.Deltapath.deltapath.com. 86400 IN SRV 20 100 5060 backup.deltapath.com.
Similar to the above lines, the lines directly below are necessary to define the actual IP
address of the primary (primary.deltapath.com) and the backup server
(backup.deltapath.com). Two DNS A records are required in this situation to tie each domain
name to an IP address.
primary IN A 192.168.0.1
backup IN A 192.168.0.2
This would allow any phones pointed to server Deltapath.deltapath.com with DNS SRV
enabled to find the primary first, if unavailable, automatically fall back to the secondary.
By default, Deltapath will advertise both the primary and secondary server IP to the following
Polycom/Poly endpoints: Polycom/Poly VVX, SoundPoint IP/SoundStation IP, and
RealPresence Trio. However, if you are using Shared Group Appearance (SGA) and Shared Line
Appearance (SLA), these features will be inoperable during failover mode. To ensure
continuous operations of these critical features, utilize a DNS SRV record to support seamless
failover.
Follow the steps below to set up a DNS SRV record. If you have a primary and secondary
server, the steps below must be performed twice – once for the primary server and once for
the secondary server.
1. Click
2. Type DNS Record in the textbox.

Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 100

Virtual Interface
A virtual interface is an IP address that is not connected to a physical interface. Physical
interfaces have a physical component to them such as an RJ45 connector. Virtual interfaces
do not have a physical component but usually an IP address port forwarded to one of the
physical interfaces.

-- 102 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 102
Make a Connection
For a clear understanding of the Virtual interface, refer to the Alternative Network
Design. In the figure, you have a WAN IP address, 202.130.106.33 that acts as a firewall. The
firewall port forwards the WAN IP address to the Deltapath UC platform with the IP address,
192.168.0.1. The unit allows the ports to forward from outside to inside the firewall. As a
result, anything sent to 202.130.106.33 on the specified ports is automatically forwarded to
192.168.0.1. Once this setup is established, the Deltapath UC platform needs to be made
aware of this address so it can correctly and efficiently process packets it receives. Without
this connection being made, the Deltapath UC platform will misinterpret the packets it
receives from 202.130.106.33 because a connection has not been established. Until the
connection is established, the Deltapath UC platform sees itself as 10.0.0.1 or 192.168.0.1.
The virtual IP address is added in the Virtual interface dialog box to ensure a connection is
established with the Deltapath UC platform.
1. Click Virtual Interface.
2. Click Add Virtual Interface.
3. IP: Enter the IP address.
4. Click Save.
Network Bonding
Network Interface bonding supports bonding of network interfaces via the Web. Companies
use network bonding to avoid a single point of failure introducing fault tolerance. If one
switch fails, another will be operational.
Example: Companies with massive data centers use network bonding to ensure their services
stay online such as a company’s phone system. If one server goes down, another takes over
seamlessly creating redundancy.

10.7 Setup QoS
This section only marks RTP and SIP packets that route through Deltapath UC. Use the same
settings in the next section to mark your endpoints. See Prepare Network for QoS for more
information on classification structures.
RTP Media Best Practice
In some setups, there are SIP gateways handling PSTN calls. As SIP negotiation and RTP
media is very straightforward, you do not need the Deltapath UC platform to handle media
relay. As a result, you can set Force RTP Packets Through Deltapath UC platform Server to
No if you are peering with a SIP PSTN gateway. This will allow RTP packets to travel directly
from an endpoint to the SIP gateway, minimizing traffic on the Deltapath UC platform server
as well as reducing a hop on the IP traffic.
However, if the foreign peer consists of a full PBX with phones, gateways, and servers, it is
possible the
• Device cannot directly route to your endpoints on your network
• Device cannot support media redirection
• Network already has QoS marking set automatically for all traffic from the Deltapath UC
platform servers. Therefore, you will want to have RTP packets to travel through the
servers to ensure quality.
In general, if the foreign SIP peer is another Deltapath UC platform or a SIP PBX with phones,
the recommended setting for Force RTP Packets Through Deltapath UC platform Server is
YES.
It is important to mention that there are certain parameters that will cause RTP to route
through the server such as the following:
• Call Recording is enabled
• Under General Settings, Yes is selected to force RTP through Deltapath server for all
users and Yes is selected to Force RTP through Deltapath server under SIP Trunk
options.
• All Deltapath Mobile calls
• Features Keys is enabled under General Setting for features such as Blind transfer and
Consultation transfer

-- 106 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 106
• Endpoints are set to outside network
• Endpoints that are behind a NAT
• SIP trunk connection that are behind NAT
Exclude the Following Networks from Session Border Controller (SBC) Network address
Translation (NAT) Processing
Certain SIP Trunk providers may provide you with a dedicated router placed in your network
with an internal IP address such as 10.x.xx, 192.168.x.x, or 172.16-31.x.x and request you add
a static route to route their SIP server WAN IP through the dedicated router. As a result, you
will need to add a special configuration, which involves excluding networks from SBC NAT
processing found under the Network tab.
To further understand the special configuration required and the information that needs to be
excluded in the Exclude the following networks from SBC NAT processing field, see the figure
and explanation below.
10.0.0.1 202.130.106.33
Internet
Deltapath UC Platform	
LAN

11 Network Security
11.1 Dynamic Filtering
Dynamic filtering protects your network from unauthorized traffic/access. It is possible to
configure Dynamic filtering rules based on the following categories: SSH Access, Distributed
Denial of Service (DDOS), Provisioning Server, SIP Server, and HTTP/HTTPS.
Dynamic filtering is enabled by default. Deactivate dynamic filtering by clicking the
Deactivate button. By default, the following settings are enabled to permanently block an
attacker’s IP.
Dynamic Filtering
SSH Access 10 attempts 10 seconds
DDOS 10 attempts 20 seconds
Provisioning
Server
10 attempts 10 seconds
SIP Server 5 attempts 20 seconds
Table 20: Dynamic Filtering
To apply custom dynamic filtering rules, follow the steps below:
1. Click
2. Type Dynamic Filtering Settings in the textbox.
3. Click Dynamic Filtering Settings.
4. Automatically block an attacker’s IP address: Enter email addresses that will be notified
anytime an IP address is blocked.
5. Always Allow Ips/CIDRs: Ensure important IP addresses are never blocked by entering
them here. See the CIDR table for more information.
6. Apply filtering rules. Example: Under each field such as SSH Access, SIP server,
HTTP/HTTPS either block IP addresses permanently or for a set amount of time in
seconds.

-- 115 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 115
7. Click Save.
11.2 Firewall Rules
Decide where firewalls are required by assessing which networks are untrusted and which are
trusted. For example, subnets inside your corporate network should be trusted while subnets
on DMZ or directly attached to the public Internet should be untrusted. Firewalls are
deployed against networks marked untrusted.
Maximum Transmission Unit (MTU) size detection, which is based on the network MTU size, is
also supported inside Firewall Rules. This feature is automatically activated.
To create your firewall rules, follow the steps below:
1. Click
2. Type Firewall Rules in the textbox.
3. Click Firewall Rules.
4. For each network port, select one of the following:
• Trusted: Subnet is trusted. Firewall is completely disabled.
• Untrusted WAN: Subnet is facing Internet only. Applies to DMZ and directly connected
WAN. The firewall will drop all packets that have a RFC 1918 Private IP Address as the
source IP.
• Untrusted LAN: Subnet is facing internal subnet, yet it is untrusted. This usually
applies to a connection to a SIP Trunk provider’s dedicated internal network. The
firewall will permit packets with RFC 1918 Private IP Address as the source IP.

-- 116 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 116
5. Allow Ping (ICMP) on untrusted interfaces: Select Yes or No.
Note: Yes, is typically selected if company’s want to run diagnostics.
Custom Rules
These rules would apply before the server settings on trusted/untrusted interfaces.
You can create custom rules for the firewall. For example, you can open a port for specific IP
ranges and then specify the ports, protocol and interface. To add custom rules,
6. Click Add Rule.
7. Double Click the row under each field (Action, IP/CIDR Range, Description, etc.) that you
want to enter information. See the CIDR table for more information.
8. Click Save.

12 Server Synchronization - Forming a Cluster
This section provides instructions for creating and configuring a single site and a multi-site
failover cluster using Putty. Before you begin forming a cluster, ensure the IP address of the
Primary and Secondary units are correct. It is highly advisable that you contact Deltapath
Support for assistance when forming a cluster and configuring network mapping.
It is also important to note that a process is in place for incoming calls once you have a
backup server. Anytime a call goes to the secondary server, it will first verify whether or not
the primary server is up. If it is detected that the primary server is up, the call will be
rerouted to the primary server.
Always configure the cluster on the secondary unit (Hot Standby) first.
12.1 Local Cluster for Single Site Failover (IP Takeover)
Now that you know how to access the menu options in Putty, you can create a local cluster.
From the menu options screen follow the steps below:
1. Deltapath#: Type cluster.
You are now ready to form a cluster using the Cluster Management Wizard. The wizard gives
you a series of questions that must be answered to form a cluster. The instructions below are
based on the example in the screenshot.
Note: The options you select in some of the steps below will vary based on such things as the
port you configured for cluster synchronization, and the IP takeover port.
2. Please Select item (1-2): Enter 1 for Cluster Management.

= Yes, 0 = No): 0
If you select IP failover on Uplink-1, when the primary server is down then the same IP

between the primary and backup server HA-1 port.

(1 = Yes, 0 = No): 1

on the primary server port 5 will be failed over to the backup server. Connect a cable
between the primary and backup server HA-2 port.

back): 1
You have the option to enable auto rollback to the primary server as soon as it resumes
service. Note, during changes between primary and secondary units, calls may be dropped.
If this is a significant concern to you, you may opt for a manual switchback at a time when
there are no active calls.

Master UC datasheet. If Master UC Dataset is used during synchronization, it copies the
users’ settings from the primary database.

Note: It is best practice to choose yes.

the settings configured have the correct information.

12.3 Verify Setup of the Cluster

work.
Administrator UI

-- 125 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 125
In the Server Status dialog box, the cluster synchronization is Active. This indicates the
cluster synchronization was successful.
After forming the primary cluster for a local Cluster, you will no longer be able to access the
secondary as the current IP address of the secondary is the same as the primary server. For
example, the IP address of the secondary that you were able to access was in Uplink-1. After
the cluster is formed, the IP address in Uplink-1 is disabled. HA-1 copies the IP address of the
primary server. You could overcome this situation if you previously assigned an additional
management IP on Uplink-1. Note, the management IP must be on a different subnet than
the IP you assigned on other ports. For example, if you are using 192.168.0.1/255.255.255.0
as the primary service IP, you cannot use 192.168.0.2/255.255.255.0 as the management IP as
this will cause unpredictable behavior.
Putty: CLI interface
From the menu options in Putty, enter the following to check cluster synchronization status.
1. Deltapath #: Type Status.

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 126
The information below references the screenshot above.
Server role: Is the server configured correctly? Example: If you were attempting to configure
a secondary, but the server role is configured as a primary then something is incorrect.
UPLINK ports configuration: In reference to the Deltapath unit, Pair 1 refers to Port 3 and
Port 4 and Pair 2 refers to Port 5 and Port 6. The state of your configuration can either be
Active or Bypass (failover).
UPLINK port current status: Identify the current state of the ports, which can be either
Active or Bypass (failover).
Uplink ports auto fallback: If Disabled and the primary fails over to the secondary, auto
fallback will not take place when the primary is restored. Use the switchcluster command
from the Command Line interface to force a fallback. You decide when you want to go back.
Disabling auto fallback ensures that active calls are never dropped because you can schedule
the fallback after business hours. Alternatively, you can Enable auto fallback.
Switchcluster Command: Control to manually switch the primary call service function
whether on primary or to secondary unit when configured in IP Take-over Cluster Mode.

timeout

Codec Negotiation Turning on codec negotiation will
allow Deltapath UC platform to
compare the codecs supported for
both endpoints during call setup.
Deltapath UC platform will
automatically select the most
preferred codec that is supported
by both endpoints. Disabling this
feature will force Deltapath UC
platform to always use the first
preferred codec and perform
codec transcoding if one of the
endpoint does not support it.
In almost all cases, turn on codec
negotiation unless you have
endpoints that can’t support SIP
re-INVITES or you are certain there
isn’t a single common codec
supported across all your end-
points.

13.2 SSL Settings for HTTPS Connections
SSL is the backbone for secure Internet. It protects sensitive information as it travels across
the Internet. Information is encrypted with an SSL certificate, which ensures sensitive data
such as credit card numbers and passwords are unreadable to everyone except the destination
server. In addition, SSL certificates also provide authentication, which ensures the
information you are sending is going to the right server. The remainder of this section details
how to set up an SSL certificate so you can enforce secure connections and establish
authentication.
Two approaches exist for issuing an SSL Certificate. Both are discussed below.
1. Use an SSL Provider: Trusted Certificate Authorities (CA) issue SSL certificates to
companies, but not before the company has gone through rigorous identity checks.
Certain types of SSL certificates require more validation than others.
2. Use a Self-Signed Certificate: You can sign and approve your own SSL certificate.
Although self-signed SSL certificates implement full encryption, visitors to your site
receive a browser alert that indicates the certificate was not verified by a trusted CA
authority and requires users to specifically trust the certificate. Be aware that this
warning can drive users away.
To obtain a SSL certificate, the first step is generating a Certificate Signing Request (CSR).
Follow the steps below to obtain a CSR, which must be given to a CA authority for approval
before you can get an SSL certificate.
1. Click
2. Type SSL Settings in the textbox.
3. Click SSL Settings.

-- 146 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 146
4. Always Use Secure Connection for HTTP (HTTPS): Yes
Note: If http is entered in a browser, HTTPs will be forced to take its place.
5. Enter all the required information under the Common Information section of the SSL
Settings dialog box.
6. Click OK. The SSL Settings are updated.
7. Click OK.
8. Next, click the tab next to the SSL Settings tab, Local Server (Primary).

The CSR is required by the CA before verification can be done and before you can receive
a server certificate file and a CA certificate file by email. Once you have both files saved,
upload the files to enable HTTPS.
2. Upload Server Certificate File (.crt): Click browse and follow the prompts to obtain the
CRT file.
3. Upload Server Private Key (.key): Click browse and follow the prompts to obtain the
Private Key file.
Some CA authorities generate an SSL certificate file and also generate a
separate private key file. If a private key file is provided, you must upload
it. If a private key was not provided, ignore step 3.
4. Click Upload.
5. Upload CA Certificate File (.ca bundle): Click browse and follow the prompts to obtain
the CA file.
6. Click Upload.
Verify you are uploading the correct CRT file. Uploading the incorrect file will
disable your web interface. If this occurs, contact Deltapath Support.

16.4 Group Permission Tab
There are three privilege tiers: Super, Manager, and Limited. Assign a tier to each ACL group
based on what the group is allowed to do by virtue of who they are. Each tier defines the
depth of access a user has and what tier they can manage.
Figure 10: Privilege Levels Associated with ACL Groups
Super Privileges: This ACL group has the highest access level. As a result, any users with
super privileges can view, modify, and change user settings within the co-super privileges and
those users under Manager and limited. Users with Super privileges can upgrade/downgrade
user’s ACL privileges to co-Super, Manager, and limited.
Note: Co-Super refers to users/accounts on the same ACL privilege as Super Privilege.
This privilege should be reserved for administrators.
Manager: This ACL group can modify and view other user settings (e.g., extensions, number
settings, etc.) with Limited and its co-Manager, but this group cannot view or modify
individuals with super-privileges. In addition, anyone under the same manager privileges

-- 157 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 157
cannot modify or upgrade their own or other co-managers’ ACL privilege to Super-Privileges.
Manager privileges can upgrade users with limited privilege to Manager privilege only.
Note: Co-Manager refers to users/accounts on the same ACL privilege as Manager Privilege.
Limited: This group has limited privileges and cannot modify or view other user settings (e.g.,
extensions, number settings, etc.) with Manager, Super and its co-limited privileges. In
Addition, anyone under this privilege cannot modify, view other users or upgrade their own
ACL privilege to Manager or Super-Privileges.
Default Permission: Click Deny All to deny all access to ACL group users or Allow All to allow
all access to the ACL group users.
When defining permissions for any ACL Group, it is best practice to first Deny All
Permissions and then selectively and carefully add specific permissions.
Allowed IP Address: Select Any which included WAN and LAN, LAN Only, and WAN Only
If your system allows exposure to the public internet, you may want to add extra security by
deciding which users, based on their privileges, can login via the WAN, LAN, or both. For
example, many companies may feel safer if they deny users with super privileges, who have
rights to everything, the ability to login from the WAN. Super privilege users can be restricted
in this case to LAN only login. If a user does proceed to try and login, they will see the
following message:

17 Contexts and Permission Groups
An administrator can further set the access level for each user by setting up a permission
group and context. Every user must be assigned to a permission group that contains a
context. A user can belong to more than one Permission Group but should only belong to one
context. Every number created must be mapped with a context. A number can have more
than one context.
When a user calls a number, Deltapath UC platform will find a number to match inside each
context of the user’s permission group. Context will be searched in the order of context
priority. The call will only be routed if there is a match. If no match is found the Deltapath
UC platform will notify the user that the call cannot be completed.
17.1 Call Permission Control and Call Admission Control
In any telephone system, it is important to enforce permission controls over the type of
numbers a user can dial. Proper permission settings are important for many reasons:
• For privacy, you want the CEO’s extension to be barred from receiving phone calls
from external.
• You want to restrict lobby phones from calling any outside numbers.
• You want to ensure your Interactive Voice Response system does not allow callers to
use your company’s telephone circuit to call outside.
• You want to ensure callers who request fax-on-demand do not request your company
to send to premium rate numbers (1-900) or international numbers.
There are 2 components relating to call permissions:
• Context: A Context identifies and categorizes a number/extension. Each
number/extension can only have 1 context attached to it. Typical names used for
contexts are Internal Extensions (default), PSTN-Local, PSTN-Domestic, and PSTN-
International.
• Permission Group: It is comprised of all the Contexts the user is permitted to call.
Based on the context and its priority, the system looks in the order of priority to see
whether there is a match with the dialed number. If there is a match, the number is
called. If there is no match, the call is disconnected.
Guarding Against Toll Fraud: Toll fraud is the use of telecommunications products or services
without the intention to pay. Employees and non-employees can gain unauthorized access to
your phone system and make long-distance calls, which can expose companies to high
expenses. You are especially vulnerable if you have a PSTN or SIP network.
Examples of Toll Fraud include:
• Long-distance and international service theft, in which employees misuse the voice
resources available for personal use.
• Long-distance and international service theft, in which non-employees who are on
location steal voice service by making long distance or international calls.

-- 168 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 168
• External parties discover misconfiguration of your Interactive Voice Response system
and manage to call out to any external numbers from your system.
• Hackers gain access to your voice network and places calls to a premium rate (revenue
sharing) number.
Every number you create must be mapped to a context, which is tied to a permission group.
The permission group defines what each context can and cannot do. Deltapath UC platform is
shipped with two default contexts and permissions.
• Context = Internal Extensions and Permission Group = Internal Only
• Context = PSTN and Permission Group = Full Access
You can define as many contexts as you find necessary. Typical contexts include local calls
and international calls.
Example: Your company wants to introduce lobby phones that can only call internal
extensions such as security and building maintenance. The Event Team that plans the annual
College Employment Recruitment event needs to make domestic calls in the USA. Lastly, the
HR department regularly dials international numbers to speak to international graduates
seeking employment with your company.
In this example, 3 different groups of users are defined. As a result, three different contexts
and permission groups need to be defined.
Lobby Phones:
Event Team:

Decreasing toll fraud is not just about selecting the right permission group, it is
equally about how you configure outbound routing. If you selected internal
Extensions as the context for dialing out to PSTN, then you would be vulnerable to
toll fraud.

Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 170
4. Click Add Permission Group.
5. Title: Enter the Permission Group title. Example: USA Domestic Calls
Note: Spaces in between words are not permitted. Use “_“ or “_” between words.
6. Description: This field is optional
7. Customer: Click the dropdown arrow and then assign a customer to the permission group.
Note: The customer will only have access to this permission group.
8. Include Context: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click the Context you will associate
with the permission group.
Multiple contexts can belong to a single permission group. When more than one context is
added to a permission group, a priority is established based on the order you select the
context in from the dropdown list. Example: When the number 450XXXX is dialed, the number
will be checked against the context with priority level 1 first and if no match is found, then
the number is checked against the priority level 2 context until the number has been verified
against all contexts. If a caller goes through all contexts they are permitted to dial and no
match is found, then a 404 message appears that communicates that the number was not
found.
17.4 Steps to Reduce Toll Fraud
Administrators must increase measures to minimize fraud and reduce the threat against
communications fraud by taking measures at the system level such as defining contexts,
permission groups, and user access.
• Ensure you do not let callers into your Interactive Voice Response (IVR) system to
enter any phone numbers. If you wish to permit a caller to enter the extension of
the employee they wish to reach, be sure to select a Permission Group that limits
the caller to only call employees’ internal extensions and not any outside numbers.
• Any phones in public areas should not be permitted to call out to external numbers
18 H.323 Gatekeeper
A gatekeeper is an optional component in the H.323 network that is often used to provide a
number of services such as endpoint registration and address resolution. If you decide to use
an H.323 gatekeeper, ensure it is configured before you create users since you would be
unable to register users’ endpoints to the gatekeeper.
Deltapath UC platform is bundled with a H.323 gatekeeper for backward compatibility with
legacy H.323 only video endpoints. The H.323 gatekeeper allows untrusted devices (devices
on the internet) to dial into your network using H.323 as well as extension to extension
dialing within your trusted devices. One thing to note, H.323 gatekeeper is not a translator. If

-- 171 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 171
your device only supports H.323 then you will only be able to call another device that
supports H.323. Some equipment such as Polycom HDX series, RealPresence Group series, and
RMX support both H.323 and SIP. These devices can register to the Deltapath UC platform
H.323 Gatekeeper and the Deltapath UC platform through SIP concurrently.
1. Click
2. Type Gatekeeper in the textbox.
3. Click H.323 Settings.
4. Gatekeeper External IP: Input the internet routable IP address attached to the Deltapath
UC platform so the internal Session Boarder Controller can process H.323 external calls to
internal calls.
Specifically, if you used the Typical Configuration, you will need to enter the assigned WAN
IP address in this box. If you use the Alternative Configuration, you will need to enter the
WAN IP address that is port forwarding to the Deltapath UC platform into this box. This is the
same IP used in Deltapath UC’s Virtual interface.
18.1 Map Conference Room Number Range to H.323 Gatekeeper
Traditionally, when users dial a Polycom video endpoint, the number is lengthy. An example
is 192.168.77.13##1004, where ##1004 represents the room number or extension. You can
map your conference room number range to the H.323 Gatekeeper to allow users to simply
dial by room number (extension 1004), which routes the user to the endpoint connected to
1004.
Follow the procedures below to create the mapping.
1. Click
2. Type Gatekeeper in the textbox.
3. Click H.323 Routing.
4. Click Add. The H.323 Routing dialog box opens.
Use the table titled, H.323 Routing Options below to complete the information in the
H.323 Routing dialog box.

19 User Profile
Define numerous user profiles before creating users. User profiles can be thought of as
templates where different templates are assigned to different groups of users. When there is a
need to make changes to a group of users, it can easily and quickly be completed by modifying
the settings inside the user profile instead of modifying each user one at a time.
Note: You must create at least one profile before creating any users in the Deltapath system.
Each user profile covers all aspects of account settings from permission group to call
preferences. To set up a user profile, follow the steps below.

-- 174 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 174
1. Click
2. Type User Profile in the textbox.
3. Click User Profile.
4. Click Add Profile to add a new profile.
5. Profile Name: Enter a profile name. Example: Standard Users.
6. Description: Enter a description about the user profile. Although this field is optional it is
highly recommended that it be used. Example: All remote employees are assigned this
profile.
Skype for Business Call
7. Skype for business Call: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click Use Skype for business
Audio Gateway or Use Skype for business Video Gateway.
Note: This step is required if you have purchased the Skype for Business module. Consult
the 0365 Connector Administrator Guide.
19.1 Profile Settings
8. ACL Group: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click an ACL Group.
9. Permission Group: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click a permission group.
10. Call Restriction: Click the dropdown arrow and Click No. This means no call is restricted
and both public switched telephone network (PSTN) and voice over IP (VOIP) calls are
supported on a single system.
• YES: Enable call control of both PSTN and VOIP. System can identify the type of call
that is currently running and will not accept a second call if the current call does
not match with the current one.
• NO: Call restriction is not enabled. The trunk will be able to mix calls (VoIP + PSTN).
• PSTN: The trunk only supports PSTN. Users cannot receive or make a call tagged as
VoIP.
• VOIP: The trunk only supports VoIP. Users cannot receive or make a call tagged as
PSTN.
Note: Logical partitioning, and therefore call restriction applies in some countries like India.
For more information, see Logical Partitioning.
11. Call Recording: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click Enable or Disable call recordings.
This option is only available if the Call Recording module is enabled.

19.5 User Profile: Schedules and Time Slots
Once you have created a user profile, you can create a customized schedule, which impacts
all users associated with the user profile. There is also a default number status that comes
out-of-the-box. The default number status is set to ring the user for 60 seconds. Remember,
timeslots can be linked to any number status. However, a number status can operate without
a timeslot. As such, timeslots are optional.

21 Schedules and Time Slots
21.1 Personal Schedules
Users can set schedules for their phones that reflect their personal work schedule. Schedules
contain rules and policies that dictate what happens to call when a particular schedule is
activated or deactivated. A personal schedule can be operated in Simple Mode or Advanced
mode.
Simple Mode and Advance Mode
In Simple Mode, there is no timeslot in place that automates schedules. Instead, you would
toggle to the schedule that fits you at the time. These schedules are especially useful for
users because they can change their schedule on an on-demand basis.
For example, users can have a schedule (number status) for Office Hour that rings their desk
phone, and an After-Office Hours schedule (number status) that rings their mobile number.
Users can set their extension to Office Hour mode for as long as they like and then change it
to After-Office Hour mode when they are ready.
On the other hand, users and administrators can create multiple schedules with timeslots to
help work more effectively and efficiently. This is Advanced mode, where all schedules are
automated. In Advanced mode, more than one schedule can run because each number status
is connected to a time slot. See the example below:
➢ An administrator creates a global schedule for all employees’ desk phone to ring
between 8 am – 6 pm.
➢ Peter, an employee, creates an After-Hour schedule that operates between the hours
of 6 – 9 pm where all calls are forwarded to his smartphone.
Note: It is best practice for administrators to create at least one Global Time Slot.
Consult the Deltapath UC User Guide for steps on creating a Simple and Advanced schedule
under My Schedule.
21.2 Time Slots in a Multitenant Environments
If you are serving many customers because you have a multitenant environment, time slots
can be dedicated to a specific customer.
1. Click
2. Type Time Slot in the textbox.

Simple and Advanced schedule under My Schedule.
22 Equipment

minute before initiating the reboot to allow the changes to update.

• Use Standard Cisco Phone Configuration: Select enable or disable. Best Practice is to

• DND Control: Click the dropdown arrow and click one of the following:
• Off by default: DND (Do Not Disturb) button is displayed on the phone, but it is not
enabled.
• On by default: DND (Do Not Disturb) button is displayed on the phone and is
enabled.
• Off permanently: DND button is not displayed on the phone.
• On permanently: DND button is displayed on the phone and is always on.

Control option.
• Semi-attended Transfer: Select one of the following:
• Local Call Forwarding Enable:
• Enable: User can control call forwarding.
• Disable: User is unable to manipulate call forwarding.
• Call Hold Ring back: Click the dropdown arrow and click one of the following:
• Disabled by default: Users will not hear their phone ring when they hang up a
call while another person is on hold. Users can enable or disable the ring
setting directly on their phone.
• Enabled by default: Users will hear their phone ring when they hang up a call
while another person is on hold. Users can enable or disable the ring setting
directly on their phone.
• Disabled permanently: The feature is disabled at a system level. Users cannot
change the setting.
• Enabled permanently: The feature is enabled at a system level. Users cannot
change the setting.
• Stutter Message Waiting: Select one of the following:
• Enable: Allow transfers to be completed before remote party
answers.
• Disable: Remote party can answer before transfer is completed.

between each number you need to enter. This is the amount of time a user has to enter
the next number. Example: The dial plan settings is set to 3 seconds. User must enter
extension 1027. Enter 1 – user has 3 seconds to enter 1. Enter 0 – user has 3 seconds to
enter 0. Enter 2 – user has 3 seconds to enter 2. If you don’t enter the second, third, or
fourth number within the 3 second timeline, then the system will consider the user is
finished and complete the call.
Best Practice is 3 seconds.
2. Digit Plan: Enter the number pattern allowed to dial from the phone. The * stands for any
number.

23 Update IEEE OUI
The Update IEEE OUI tool now ensures the server can reach standards-oui.ieee.org before
downloading and overwriting the existing file. Administrators can also enter a Custom URL.
IEEE is the registration authority organization that keeps track of all the device MAC address
records. OUI is organizationally a unique identifier for the MAC address of a phone.
In some instances, you might add an endpoint MAC address, which fail to resolve to a brand.
This means the Server OUI records are not up to date. Use the steps below to update the OU
and to ensure the brand and model is recognized.
To update, do the following:

-- 202 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 202
1. Click
2. Enter System Services Management in the textbox.
3. Click System Services Management.
4. Select an update method: Select Use Default Link or Use Custom URL.
5. URL Setting: Enter the OUI URL if you selected Use Custom URL.
6. Click Submit.
7. Click OK.

Mac-based device.
The fields under Equipment for Mac-based devices are defined below.
• Type: Click Mac-based devices if you are using RealPresence Trio, Polycom VVX,
SoundStation IP or SoundPoint IP.
• MAC Address: Enter the address. The address is also known as the barcode or serial
number. It can be found on the back of your phone.
• If you assign an additional extension on a device that’s already in the system (a user
with the same MAC address), select the MAC address from the dropdown menu next to
the MAC Address field. The list is sorted by the primary User in Line 1.
• Model: Example: Polycom
• Click the dropdown arrow in the field to the right of Model and then Click the model
number. The model number is located on the phone, typically in the back.
• Line Number: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click a line number. The number you
choose determines the line your extension is displayed on from your phone screen.
Note: By default, you must select line 1 first. Unless there is an extension associated with line
one, you are unable to select the remaining lines. The extension will automatically be assigned
line 1 if you choose another line.
• UC Registration IP: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click an IP address. To ensure
you select the correct IP address, such as a public /internet or internal/private IP see
the information below.

24.2 Avaya Phones
For a complete list of phones that can be integrated with Deltapath UC, see Appendix 2.
To configure a user’s phone, find the user’s Avaya phone model and follow the steps below.
Avaya 9641G and 9611G Phones
1. Start up the phone.
2. Press the * key to enter the program.
3. Enter 27238 to access the configuration menu.
4. Under the Address menu, locate Http Server. Enter Deltapath UC platform IP address.
5. Under the SIG menu, change the setting from Default to SIP.
Avaya 4621SW and 4610SW
1. Startup phones and wait until it tells you to Press the * key.
2. Press * key.
3. Press the # key multiple times until the Enter Command is displayed
4. Press the Mute key and then enter 73738 followed by the #key.
Note: 73738 spells RESET.
5. Press the # key for Yes to confirm a phone reset. This will configure the factory settings
for the phone.

-- 206 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 206
6. Wait for phone to reboot and prompt you to press the * key.
7. Press the * key.
8. Enter all required information in the fields and then Press the # key to save the values.
9. Press the # key multiple times until the Enter Command is displayed.
10. Press the Mute key and then enter 744 followed by the #.
Note: 744 spells SIG
11. Press the * key to change the SIG value to SIP.
12. Save the new settings.
Making the Avaya phone compatible with Deltapath UC platform, will impact the following:
▪ DTMF mode will always use rfc2833
▪ 4610SW and 4621SW do not support geographic cluster (dual IP registration for
redundancy)
▪ 4621SW and 4610SW will be unable to dial numbers beginning with *. Example: The
Avaya user cannot dial *1007 which normally sends all incoming calls directly to the
call recipient’s voicemail. This occurs because
o The phone is sending a SIP INVITE without a proper To field. Example: *1007
translates to <sip:*1007>
o Dialing a number such as 4444 will work because it translates correctly, such as
sip:4444@192.168.77.36 .
▪ Similarly, 4621SW and 4610SW is unable to use * followed by a number to route to
other places such as an IVR.
▪ 4621SW and 4610SW always use UDP as the SIP Transport.
▪ 9641G and 9611G always use TCP as the SIP Transport.
▪ 4621SW and 4610SW do not support G.722 Codec.
▪ Enabling the video codec on any Avaya phone will negatively affect phone
performance. Best practice is not to enable H264.
