24.4 Manually Provision Polycom HDX and RealPresence Group
Polycom HDX and RealPresence Group can be manually provisioned or auto provisioned.
Before auto-provisioning your HDX or Group, consider the following:
• The following features are hardcoded and enabled in Polycom HDX and RealPresence
Group series: Call History, Call Detail Report, and Recent Calls, Allow Directory
Changes, Mute Auto Answer Calls, and Maximum Time in call is set to 480 min = 8 hrs.
• For successful provisioning of your HDX and Group series, see Appendix 1.
• If you choose to auto-provision your Polycom HDX or RealPresence Group series, you
cannot register to the H.323 gatekeeper. To register to both SIP and H.323, provision
manually.

Note: It is recommended to auto-provision your HDX if your environment needs to be
provisioned via an internal LAN IP Address, but the SIP registration needs to be provisioned
via a WAN IP.
Once the user is setup in the User Settings dialog box, do the following:
1. Connect to the HDX interface from a web browser.
2. Click Admin Settings. You are prompted for your administrative login and password.
3. Click Global Services.
4. Click Provisioning Service.
5. Domain: Enter the Deltapath UC IP address or FQDN the HDX will register to.
Note: Use the internal LAN IP of Deltapath UC if HDX is within your corporate network.
Otherwise, use the Deltapath UC WAN IP if the HDX is located outside your corporate
network.
6. Username: Enter the user’s extension.
7. Password: Select the checkbox.
8. Password: Enter the PIN.
9. Confirm Password: Reenter the password.
10. Server Address: Enter the same IP address as the domain IP.
11. Click Update.

24.6 Zero Touch Provisioning
Use the flowchart below to determine whether or not you qualify for Zero Touch Provisioning.
Figure 12: Different Approaches for Provisioning Endpoints
Configuring Devices Inside the Corporate Network
The Deltapath UC platform comes equipped with a DHCP server, which can be enabled or
disabled. If the following conditions are met below, your endpoints are ready to have their
configurations and firmware automatically uploaded from a DHCP server:
o Deltapath UC platform is a DHCP server and broadcasting the server IP address
o You are using a third party DHCP server and option 66 is broadcasting the Deltapath
UC’s IP address
o Endpoints are in factory default mode and directly connected to the Deltapath UC
o Deployment mode is selected under Polycom setting if using Polycom endpoints

-- 219 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 219
Note: Deployment mode only works when phones are part of the corporate network using
RFC1918 Private IP Addresses.
Once your devices are automatically provisioned, you are ready to use them.
Follow the steps below to automatically broadcast IP Addresses to endpoints if your Deltapath
UC platform is a DHCP server.
1. Click
2. Type Network in the textbox.
3. Click DHCP.
4. Broadcast Server IP in DHCP: Select Yes
Immediately after deploying phones, switch to production mode. Not switching modes
can have serious security issues.
If any of the conditions above are not met, follow the steps below to manually provision your
device.
Configuring Devices Outside the Corporate Network
If the Deltapath UC platform is not a DHCP server or the third party DHCP server is not
broadcasting Deltapath UC’s IP address in option 66 then your phones must be provisioned
manually.
When company phones such as your Polycom VVX or RealPresence Trio are located in remote
locations such as an employee’s home, deployment mode cannot be used because the phones
are not connected to the corporate network and therefore untrusted. As a result, phones
outside the corporate network must also be manually configured. The server should be in
production mode and the phone should be in factory default mode.

5. Press Enter.

9. Press Select.

web configuration.

example, configure users to follow default settings or phone language. To make changes, follow
the steps below.

3. Click Equipment.
4. Locate the extension you want to apply custom settings to and click the icon.

25 Getting Started with the VVX D60 Wireless Handset
Before you can begin using the VVX D60 Wireless Handset, your VVX business media phone must
be running UC Software 5.4.3 or later. Your VVX business media phone will automatically be
upgraded to the most current Deltapath supported Polycom UC Software version once it is
provisioned and registered to Deltapath UC. The VVX D60 feature must also be enabled on
your phone. When the feature is enabled, you can pair the VVX D60 Base Station with your
phone, then register the wireless handset with the base station.
25.1 Enable the VVX D60 Feature
Before pairing the base station with a business media phone, enable the VVX D60 feature. To
enable the VVX D60 feature, access the Advanced settings menu directly on the VVX business
media phone. If you do not have access to the Advanced Settings menu, contact your system
administrator.
To enable the VVX D60 feature:
1. On your VVX business media phone, navigate to Settings > Advanced.
2. Enter the administrator password for access to the menu.
3. Select VVX D60 Configuration.
4. Select VVX D60 Profile, then select Enable.
25.2 Pair the Base Station with the VVX Phone
When the VVX D60 feature is enabled on your VVX business media phone, you can pair your
base station with the phone. You can choose to pair the base station in the following ways:
• PC port pairing
• Automatic pairing
• Manual pairing
When the base station is paired with the phone, the Handset icon displays on the VVX business
media phone.
Note: You cannot pair a base station with a VVX business media phone when a VVX Expansion
Module is connected to the phone. Disconnect any VVX Expansion Modules before pairing the
VVX D60 base station.

-- 254 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 254
25.3 Pair with the Base Station using PC Port Pairing
When the Ethernet cable is connected from the base station LAN port to the PC port on the
VVX business media phone, the phone pairs with the base station automatically.
If for some reason the base station is no longer paired with the VVX business media phone,
you can follow the steps below to pair the base station with the phone again using PC port
pairing.
1. On the VVX business media phone, navigate to Settings > Features > VVX D60
Configuration.
2. On the VVX D60 Configuration screen, select Base Station, and then select PC Port Pairing.
25.4 Pair with the Base Station using Automatic Pairing
When an Ethernet cable is connected from the base station LAN port into a LAN outlet, all
VVX business media phones automatically display all base stations on the network as long as
the devices are on the same network. This enables you to select which base station to pair
with your phone.
1. On the VVX business media phone, navigate to Settings > Features > VVX D60
Configuration.
2. On the VVX D60 Configuration screen, select Base Station, and then select Automatic
Pairing.
3. Select a base station, then select Pair.
25.5 Pair with the Base Station using Manual Pairing
When an Ethernet cable is connected from the base station LAN port to the PC port on the
VVX business media phone or when the Ethernet cable is connected from the base station LAN
port into a LAN outlet, you can manually enter the base station IP address to pair with a
phone. Ask your system administrator for the IP address of your base station.
1. On the VVX business media phone, navigate to Settings > Features > VVX D60
Configuration.
2. On the VVX D60 Configuration screen, select Base Station, then select Manual Pairing.
3. Enter the IP address of the base station, and then select Pair.
25.6 Register the Wireless Handset
After the VVX D60 Base Station is paired with the VVX business media phone, you can register
up to five wireless handsets to the base station.
1. On the wireless handset, navigate to Menu > Settings > Features > Registration.
2. Select Register.
3. Press and hold the FIND button on the base station for a few seconds.
4. Select Yes to confirm the registration with the base station. When the registration is
successful, the message “Registration Successful” displays.

-- 255 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 255
26 Uploading A Customized Phone Logo
Customized phone logos can be uploaded to a number of phones including, Polycom, Cisco,
and Yealink endpoints.
1. Click the Deltapath icon in the sidebar of switchboard.
2. Enter logo.
3. Click Phone Logo.
4. Click Upload Logo File under the Logo File Library tab.
5. Logo Image File: Click Browse and follow the steps to select a file.
6. Click Upload. The Phone Logo Settings dialog box opens.
Note: The image specifications for the phones are located in the table below.
7. Logo Image: Click the dropdown arrow and select the image file.
8. Assigned Customer: Click the dropdown arrow and select the customer you want to
associate the logo with.
9. Device: Click the dropdown arrow and select the device the logo will be uploaded to.
10. Click OK.
11. Click OK.
Note: All uploaded phone logos will appear under the tab, Phone Logo List.
Phones will need to be rebooted. See Equipment.

27 Dolby Voice
Deltapath offers an end-to-end business communication product line that fully incorporates
Dolby Voice through its partnership with Dolby, a world-renowned company that delivers
innovative audio solutions. Users’ conference experience dramatically improves while
longstanding business communication challenges are solved with Dolby Voice:
• Voices are picked up from anywhere in a conference room and across rooms of
different sizes. Speakers can sit anywhere in a conference room and speak in a natural
voice and be heard.
• Crystal clear sound is delivered with revolutionary noise cancelling technology. Work
anywhere without compromising a meeting.
• Voice placement ensures every speaker’s voice emerges from a distinct location
making conversations easy to follow. Participants’ voices never overlap when
participants speak all at once.
• Deltapath UC platform audio conference bridge also extends the Dolby Voice
experience when participants use a Dolby endpoint. Participants that are not using a
Dolby endpoint will have the additional benefit of noise cancellation as they interact
on a call.
• The reach of Dolby Voice extends beyond a conference bridge to mobile teams and
inter-office teams (Deltapath UC platform to Deltapath UC platform)
o When users use Deltapath Mobile, Deltapath’s mobility client, and dial a
Deltapath Mobile number, users experience all the benefits of Dolby Voice.
o A call between Deltapath Mobile and a Dolby endpoint, also delivers all the
benefits of Dolby Voice.
o If a Dolby endpoint joins an audio conference hosted on Deltapath’s Deltapath
UC platform, both endpoints experience Dolby Voice.
For further discussion about Dolby Voice, contact Deltapath Sales.
27.1 Dolby Conference Endpoint
Deltapath UC platform transforms your Dolby Conference device into a robust phone solution.
• With Deltapath UC, your Dolby Conference Device can receive calls and make
outbound calls.
• Deltapath UC integrates different communication platforms so users can talk to others
on different endpoint brands such as Cisco, Avaya, etc.
• Are you hosting a conference? With the Dolby Conference phone, you can enjoy one
touch to join a conference right from the phone.
• Call Skype for Business users right from the Dolby conference phone. Enter a Skype for
business email address.
• The Dolby conference phone can be connected to any legacy or IP based telephone
system. No need to replace your old system.
For further discussion about Dolby Conference endpoints, contact Deltapath Sales. If you
already have a Dolby Conference endpoint, add the Dolby endpoint to Deltapath UC.

28.1 How To Use Call Forwarding
Once the call forwarding number is set up and communicated to users, they can dial the call
forwarding number. The system will request users to enter the destination number they want
to forward calls to when call forwarding is activated. The number they enter is typically
followed by the # key. Example: 3254#

28.2 How To Cancel Call Forwarding
To cancel call forwarding, users need to dial the call forwarding number again. This action
toggles call forwarding to the off mode.
28.3 Setup A Do Not Disturb Number
To set up a DND number or code, follow the same steps above for setting up call forwarding.
However, in step 13 select Toggle Do Not Disturb. Once DND is activated, callers will be sent
to voicemail. For compatible desk phones with a DND button, the DND button illuminates
when DND is activated.

29.2 Configuring the IP Address of the Gateway via Console Access
You require the console cable that comes with the accessories pack and a USB to Serial
adapter (not included) to connect to your computer. You also require a console terminal such
as Putty. Follow the steps below.
M800
1. Launch the Putty application.
2. Select Connection Type: Serial.
3. Serial line: Enter the Com Port that your console cable is connected to.
Use your Windows Device Manager to discover what Com Port you are using.
4. Click Serial, which is located under the Category window and configure Putty based on the
settings below:
• Baud Rate: 115,200 bps
• Data Bits: 8
• Parity: None
• Stop Bits: 1
• Flow Control: None
5. Click Open and login with the pre-set gateway administrator credentials as shown on the
license paper.

7. Type your password again.
8. Type the following: conf voip
9. Type the following: interface network-if 0
10. Type the following: set ip-address 192.168.78.252.
Replace the IP address with your own. 192.168.78.252 is an example only.
11. Type the following: set prefix-length Example 22 to define the subnet mask.
Use the chart below to find your prefix length. 22 is an example only.
12. Type the following: set gateway 192.168.78.253.
Enter the new default gateway IP address. 192.168.78.253 is an example only.
13. Type the following: activate
14. Type the following: do reload now
The gateway will reboot and boot up with the network assignment you entered above.

29.3 Configuring the IP Address of the Gateway via Telnet Access
You can also telnet to the gateway if you do not have a USB to Serial Adapter by connecting
your computer network port to the Ethernet port of the gateway.
M800
The M800 gateway series has 4 Ethernet ports. Use port 1 in the M800 series. To telnet into the
gateway, plug the computer into the Ethernet port of the gateway.
1. Assign your computer an IP address of 169.254.0.4.
2. Use Putty in Telnet mode to connect to the default IP address of the gateway,
169.254.0.3.
3. Click Open and login with your administrator credentials.
4. Type the following: En
5. Click Open and login with the pre-set gateway administrator credentials as shown on the
license paper.
6. Type the following: En
7. Type your password again.
8. Type the following: conf voip
9. Type the following: interface network-if 0
10. Type the following: set ip-address 192.168.78.252.
Note: Replace the IP address with your own. 192.168.78.252 is an example only.
11. Type the following: set prefix-length
Note: Use the CIDR table below to find your prefix length. For example, the prefix
length for 255.255.255.0 is 22. .
12. Type the following: set gateway 192.168.78.253.
Note: Enter the new default gateway IP address. 192.168.78.253 is an example only.
13. Type the following: activate
14. Type the following: do reload now
The gateway will reboot and boot up with the network assignment you entered above.

29.8 Custom Routing
By default, when a number is dialed, Deltapath UC platform sends the number to the gateway
and the gateway sends the number to a PSTN provider as entered on a keypad. You may
however, discover you have specialized routing needs.
Digit Manipulation
In custom routing, you can also manipulate the numbers dialed before sending the number to
the gateway.
To do this, you would need to add an AudioCodes gateway, select FXO or PRI/BRI models and
then select Advance Routing.
Example 1: If the number dialed is 813- 323-0112
Prefix: 813
Replacement: 011813
The number sent to the TDM side is 011- 813- 323-0112

-- 275 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 275
Example 2: If the number dialed is 408-534-2424
Prefix: 408
Replacement: Field is left empty
The number sent to the TDM side is 534-2424
Example 3: If the number dialed is 408-534-2424
Prefix: Field is left empty
Replacement: Filed is left empty
The number sent to the TDM side will remain unchanged as 408-534-2424
Note: If both the prefix and replacement fields are left blank, the number
dialed remains the same as it travels from the Deltapath UC platform to the
gateway and then to the PSTN provider (TDM side)
Table 38: Digit Manipulation
Prefix: Enter the number or numbers you want removed or replaced before moving to the
gateway.
Replacement: Enter a number or numbers that will replace the information entered in the
prefix field. When nothing is added to the replacement field, the number in the prefix field
is deleted as no replacement number is specified.
29.9 Preferred ISDN Port Routing by Caller ID
If you have two phone companies such as AT&T and Verizon along with two different number
ranges, you will need to route your users to the correct circuit for the phone companies. This
option will analyze the user’s caller ID to determine the correct circuit to route the calls. For
example, if you have a circuit from AT&T connecting on Port 1 with a number range of
14082351xxx and a Verizon circuit on Port 2 with a number range of 1650 7663xxx, the
gateway can match the user’s Caller ID. For example, if the outbound caller ID is
14082351xxx, route it through Port 1. This ensures the numbers dialed get routed to the
correct phone companies.
Preferred ISDN Port Routing by Caller ID: The IP phone number or range of IP phone numbers
tied to a Caller ID will be routed to the identified port. The port the number is routed to
depends on the Caller ID and not the actual number dialed.

-- 276 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 276
For example: If a user dials 408-545-2300 and the Caller ID starts with 3678 then the gateway
sends the call to port 1. If 408-545-2300 is dialed and the Caller ID number starts with 3679
then the gateway sends the call to port 2. If there is no match, the * which means anything
tells the gateway to use port 1.
29.10 Preferred ISDN Port Routing by Destination:
Make a Connection
To explain Preferred ISDN Port routing by Destination, Gateway Interfacing with a
Legacy PBX is referenced as this feature is primarily used in the situation discussed. Based on
this figure, it is assumed that port 2 connects to a legacy PBX and Port 1 connects to a
telephone company. As a result, you will want to dictate where the numbers dialed are
directed, Port1 or Port 2. To do this, there are three steps.
• Step 1: Configure outbound routing and prepend digits in the outbound dialog box for
each number range. For more information, see Outbound Routing. Example: Prepend
02 to the number range to identify the number as belonging to Port 2. The steps are
repeated for the number ranges that will be sent to the telephone company. 01 is
prepended to the number range to identify the number as belonging to Port 1
(telephone company).
• Step 2: Match the prepended numbers to the ports they belong to. Example: Under
Preferred ISDN, define the numbers that should be matched to Port 1 and Port 2,
which in this example is 01 and 02.
• Step 3: Now that you have designated 01 and 02 to port 1 and port 2, you need to use
custom routing to strip the prepended digits since you do not want these special
prefixes 01 and 02 to be sent to the PSTN provider or Legacy PBX. See Custom Routing.
Enter 01 and 02 under the Prefix field and leave Replacement blank.
29.11 IP Security
This feature should always be enabled so the built-in firewall is activated on the gateway. Once
enabled, only the SIP servers added to the list will be able to send calls to the gateway.
1. Click Add IP address/Host.
2. Enter the IP address assigned to the Deltapath UC.
29.12 Configure PRI/BRI Telephone Circuit (Ports)
In this section, you will configure Port 1 and Port 2. If you only have 1 line to connect to with
a 2 Port gateway, ensure you use Port 1. If you need to connect to both PSTN provider and a
legacy PBX, use Port 1 for the carrier/network and Port 2 for PBX.
It is mandatory for you to use port one first before you can use port 2.

-- 277 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 277
1. Name: Enter the name of the entity you are assigning to the port. Example AT&T
Telecommunications company.
2. Orientation: The customer is the user, and the telephone company is the network.
Recommended default is User.
Note: The options are given because there are times when the user sometimes can act as
a network and therefore, needs to set the port to Network.
3. Protocol: The protocol used depends on your phone company. Find out the protocol your
phone company is using and select from the dropdown list.
4. Framing Method: Speak with your phone company to identify your framing method and then
choose it from your dropdown list.
5. Relay Caller Name Display: If your phone company supports call name display, call
recipients will see callers’ names when set to Yes.
6. CRC: It is an error checking protocol. Whether or not it is enabled depends on your phone
company.
7. Overlap Dial: Only enabled when interfacing with a legacy PBX. Recommended default is
No.
8. Click Next when you have completed this dialog box to open the final dialog box.

Deltapath UC supports MS Hyper-V and VMWare for Skype for Business Gateway.
SFB Video Gateway is a transcoder between Microsoft’s Skype for Business (formerly Microsoft
Lync) and virtually any enterprise videoconferencing endpoints. With SFB Video Gateway
customers can interact with external parties including:
• Legacy H.323 Video Conferencing Systems
• Open SIP Video Conferencing Systems
• Web Browsers
• SIP Mobile Clients

A gateway’s location logically separates multiple integrated MCUs according to their function.
It allows intelligent decisions to be made about routing media, signaling, and overflow
function when a particular MCU’s resources reach maximum capacity.
A minimum of 2 locations are required to setup an SFB Video Gateway:
• One for internal Connections
• One for external Connections

Integrated MCU Tab
Deltapath’s Deltapath Skype for Business Video Gateway can host conferences. As a result, it
functions as a Multipoint Control Unit (MCU). To provision, one of the integrated MCU must be
provisioned for internal (LAN) access while the second integrated MCU must be provisioned
for external (WAN) access, which will typically be used to interface with MS LYNC/MS SFB.

perspective to ensure all required ports have been redirected to the correct MCU to enable it
to function at full capacity.

30.5 Skype for Business Video Gateway Conference Capability
Besides interfacing with Skype for Business, the SFB Video Gateway has another significant
benefit. It also acts as a video conferencing bridge that allows participants to use standard
SIP, SFB, H.323, and WebRTC (Web Browser) endpoints to call into a conference.
In addition, you can set up a Gateway VMR or a Virtual Hall conference. If you expect all or a
large number of participants to contribute their thoughts during the meeting, then set up a
VMR conference so everyone is on video. If you are holding a seminar or other similar event
where participants will be listening to the speaker with a specified period for questions and
answers, then you should set up a Virtual Hall conference. Only the speaker will appear on
the video. The other main difference between the Gateway VMR and the Virtual Hall is the
Virtual Hall has two additional settings:
• Mute all guests: Only the speaker can be heard during the conference.
• Lock presenter as main speaker: As the main focal point and speaker, the presenter’s
video screen is enlarged compared to meeting participants’ screen.

-- 293 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 293
30.6 Setup Virtual Meeting: Room VMR or Virtual Hall
There are two types of VMR and Virtual Hall users:
➢ Host – The owner of the VMR. A host can manage a meeting by inviting participants,
locking a conference to prevent late participants from entering the conference, mute
and unmute participants, and disconnect participants.
➢ Attendee – Attendees, also known as participants, are people that join a conference.
Attendees do not have the ability to manage a conference.
1. Click Settings.
2. Click Services Setup.
3. Click the VMR tab or the Virtual Hall tab and then click Add.

30.8 Routing Rules
Routing rules are a set of rules that allow inbound and outbound calls from other endpoints
that are not registered to Deltapath UC. When you define routing rules, you help Skype for
Business Gateway’s MCU conduct conferences and communicate with Skype for Business/Lync
users.

Sample Routing and Scenario
Routing a call from Skype for Business / Lync client that will be sent by dialing a name or
number with the domain name “deltapath.com” to Deltapath UC with IP address of
192.168.78.16.
On the INCOMING portion of the routing rule, enter the fixed domain value you are expecting
from Microsoft (deltapath.com). The variable is placed or matched inside this symbol (. +).
On the OUTGOING portion, enter the value “\1” which means it will only retain whatever is
inside (.+) and add 192.168.78.16 (IP of Deltapath UC).

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 302
Sample of a Cloud SFB Configuration
Assume in this example the TLS certificate has been uploaded to the gateway.
conference00.example.com is used as the valid FQDN. The MSSIP domain based from the
assumption is example.com.
On your DNS servers, add SRV record for your MS SFB/Lync federation:
“_sipfederationtls._tcp.example.com. 1799 IN SRV 1 100 5061 conference00.example.com.”
With this SRV record in place, it allows Microsoft to send all traffic that uses port 5061 with
the domain example.com to send over to conference00.example.com which is your
integrated MCU.
DNS SRV Records:
“_sipfederationtls._tcp.example.com. 1799 IN SRV 1 100 5061
conference00.example.com.”
where:
sipfederationtls. –
standard SIP TLS
Federation
_tcp. - transport
protocol of the
desired service
example.com – valid
domain name
1779 – DNS query
expiry (TTL)
IN – Standard Class
Field (always IN)
SRV – stands for
Service Record
1 - the priority of the target
host, lower value means more
preferred.
100 - relative weight for
records with the same
priority, higher value means
more preferred.
5061 – TCP/UDP port used (for
TLS should always 5061)
Conference00.example.com –
target (the canonical
hostname of the machine
providing the service)

Sample of On-Premise SFB Configuration Application Pool
1. Configure a trusted application pool in the SFB/LYNC system for the integrated MCU
Legend:
"conferencenodeFQDN" = meet.hk.deltapath.com

meet.hk.deltapath.com -Registrar lyncserver.deltapath.com -Site 1 -RequiresReplication

*example:

meet.hk.deltapath.com -Port 5061
3. Must configure a static SIP domain route and associate this with a trusted application
*example:
New-CsStaticRoutingConfiguration -Identity "Service:Registrar: lyncserver.deltapath.com "
$newroute = New-CsStaticRoute -TLSRoute -Destination " meet.hk.deltapath.com " -Port
5061 -MatchUri "deltapath.com" -UseDefaultCertificate $true
Set-CsStaticRoutingConfiguration -Identity "Service:Registrar:lyncserver.deltapath.com" -
Route @{Add=$newroute}
4. Client must enable the topology
*example:
Enable-CsTopology

DNS SRV Records Requirements
Skype for Business gateway is designed to support SIP and H.323 services. This is supported
by adding SRV records for SIP and H.323 to the DNS server.
Example:
The integrated MCU has the FQDN: meet.deltapath.com. We want to enable SIP and H.323
services directly to and from the gateway.
Domain Service Protocol Priority Weight Port Target Host
deltapath.com. H323ls UDP 10 10 1719 meet.deltapath.com.
deltapath.com. H323cs TCP 10 10 1720 meet.deltapath.com.
deltapath.com. SIP TCP/UDP 10 10 5060 meet.deltapath.com.
deltapath.com. SIPS TCP 10 10 5061 meet.deltapath.com.
For H323 support:
_h323ls._udp.deltapath.com. 1799 IN SRV 10 10 1719 meet.deltapath.com.
_h323cs._tcp.deltapath.com. 1799 IN SRV 10 10 1720 meet.deltapath.com.
For SIP support:
_sip._tcp.deltapath.com. 1799 IN SRV 10 10 5060 meet.deltapath.com.
_sips._tcp.deltapath.com. 1799 IN SRV 10 10 5061 meet.deltapath.com.
30.10 Interfacing SFB / Lync Client with Deltapath Skype for Business
Gateway
Manage outgoing PSTN calls from SFB / Lync client. Enable Account Mapping to ensure
SFB/Lync Client users with the specified domain name are only allowed to make PSTN Calls.
There are 2 types of mapping:
USER based - For user based mapping, Deltapath UC follows the owner’s permission group. As
a result, all dialing rules of the mapped Deltapath UC owner is replicated when the owner is
using his SFB/Lync client to dial a PSTN number and displays his personal caller ID.
CUSTOM based - For custom based mapping a general caller ID name and number can be
displayed as well as the permission group to follow and creation of account mapping when
dialing to PSTN.
User Based Mapping

2. Type Skype for Business in textbox.
3. Click Account Mapping.
4. Click Add.

30.11 Simultaneous Ringing of Desk Phone and SFB/Lync Client with
Incoming PSTN Call
To enable simultaneous ringing, a routing rule must be defined in SFB Video Gateway. The
configuration requirement is explained below.
To implement simultaneous ringing, the character “_AT_” must be used at all times to
separate the target destination and the domain in the Destination RegExp.
Sample
An integrated MCU has the hostname, meet.deltapath.com. The SFB Client will be dialing to
a Skype for Business/Lync user with username, support@365.deltapath.com
To be able to support this, setup the following routing rule:
The gateway in this case is expecting an incoming call with a format:
(.+)_AT_(.+)@meet.deltapath.com. Deltapath UC is designed to send out a call with the
character “_AT_” Once the gateway matches the Destination RexExp, it will strip the
hostname, meet.deltapath.com, and pass it on to the Transformation RegExp. With the rule
\1@\2, the gateway will retain whatever data is contained in the 1st (.+) and replace _AT_
with @ and retain the 2nd (.+). The final output will be support@365.deltapath.com

-- 309 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 309
31 Survivability: When Is It Needed?
Organizations can experience a network outage at any time. Being caught off guard typically
means all phones become non-operational, which can severely impact business operations.
Organizations with remote branches that have their endpoints directly registered to a
headquarters (central branch), experience business interruptions when there is an internet
outage either at the central branch or the remote branch. As a result, the remote office
phone devices will not work.

-- 310 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 310
Figure 19: No Survivability in a Remote Location
Based on the screenshot above, if only the remote office link fails (192.168.0.1) the remote
offices devices will not work. Similarly, if the main link fails (202.130.106.33) the remote
office devices will also fail. Ensure it is business as usual if there is a network outage by
learning how you can achieve survivability.
If you purchased the survivability license and deployed your gateway with survivability in a
remote branch, then the gateway can take on the temporary role of a PBX in the event of an
internet outage. All devices register to the Deltapath UC platform and the PSTN gateway.
Calls coming in from the PSTN go to the gateway, which forwards the calls to the Deltapath
UC platform. The Deltapath UC platform then forwards the incoming call to the correct
extension. If there is a network outage that prevents the phones from connecting to the
Deltapath UC platform, survivability emergency mode is triggered on the gateway. Once in
survivability mode, the devices in the remote office can call each other, and users in the
remote office can make and receive calls to ensure a company remains operational. The
remote office cannot, however, make or receive calls from headquarters. The process is
seamless. Equally important, companies benefit from survivability mode because they know
important operations remain normal for end users which buys them time to troubleshoot the
network issue.

-- 311 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 311
Figure 20: Establishing Survivability with a PSTN Gateway in a Remote Location
It is important to understand when survivability emergency mode is activated, Deltapath UC
platform is unavailable. This means all features and services that are usually offered are
unavailable, including but not limited to CDR reports, IVR, Call Center, and Billing reports.
Equally important, in the event the primary server fails in a multi-site cluster solution,
phones will not register on the secondary server since the phones only register to the primary
server and the gateway.
Once the phones connect to the Deltapath UC platform, survivability emergency mode
deactivates within 180 seconds and normal operations resume.
Configure Survivability
Once the survivability license is purchased, enable survivability. From the Deltapath
administrator UI, do the following:
Gateway Settings
1. Click
2. Type AudioCodes.
3. Click AudioCodes.
4. Click modify, which is located to the right of the gateway you want to modify.
5. Select Advanced Settings.
6. Select Enable Mediant 800 (M-800) gateway if it is not selected.

-- 312 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 312
7. Click Next.
8. Click Next.
9. Click OK.
When Survivability is enabled, two SIP trunks with the same gateway IP address are
automatically created. Go to System Status >> Active Peer List. You will notice two peers
with the same IP address, but different ports (UDP 5060 and 5070). This is a normal behavior
when survivability is enabled for a gateway.
User Settings

2. Type User.

Note: If you are adding users for the first time, see Add Users.
5. Scroll down to Equipment.

7. Click OK.
31.1 Polycom Video Border Proxy (VBP)
*In Deltapath UC, release 5.1, only legacy support is provided for this functionality.
Polycom VBP can be used as a survivability gateway for Polycom phones registering to
Deltapath. When configured, Polycom phones will register to the Polycom VBP internal SIP
Proxy which acts as a back-to-back agent to Deltapath. Before Polycom phones can be set up
for use with VBP as the survivability gateway, define the VBP address in Deltapath by
following the steps below.
1. Click
2. Type VBP in the textbox.
3. Click Polycom VBP.
4. Click Add Polycom VBP.
5. Name: Enter a name.
6. Description: Optional field.
7. IP Address: Enter the VBP WAN interface IP address.

-- 313 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 313
Once VBP is defined, the next step is modifying the accounts of users who will use VBP as a
SIP proxy for registering to Deltapath from outside the corporate network.
1. Click
2. Type User in the textbox.
3. Click the Modify User button located to the right of the user you want to modify.
4. Scroll down to Profile settings and click the arrow to open it.
5. Click Customize.
6. Polycom VBP: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click the VBP address you defined.
7. Click OK.
The phone configuration file is generated with the VBP WAN IP address as the SIP Proxy.
Reboot phones. Ensure your phone provisioning server is pointing to Deltapath to download
the updated configuration file.

34.2 Outbound Routing – Calling via SIP Trunk / PSTN/Third Party PBX
To make a call to another SIP peer (whether it is a third-party PBX, a SIP Provider, or a PSTN
Gateway) set up outbound routing. More than one outbound route may be required. For
example, if you have a third-party PBX such as Cisco Unified Communications Manager, you
need to create an outbound route to allow extension to extension dialing to the third-party
PBX. To create another outbound route to route PSTN numbers to another peer, which could
be a third-party IP PBX, PSTN Gateway or a SIP Provider, follow the steps below to create both
outbound routes.

Number Patterns Example
Pattern: X matches any digit from 0-9

N matches any digit from 2-9
. wildcard, must have a character in
the place of the period.
! wildcard. You can insert a number
in place of the exclamation mark or

the underscore ( _ ) when the rules
contain variables indicating
patterns such as X, N, Z, . and !
_1xxx such as 1053
_1zzz such as 1153
_1zxx such as 1108
_1nnn such as 1253
_1xx.
The system will accept 123 plus
a number from 0-9 with
unlimited numbers such as 1239
or 1234567890.
The system only accepts four
digits. 123 would not be
acceptable.
_1xx!
The system will accept three
digits such as 123 or 123 plus a
number from 0-9 such as 1230 or
1235.
The system will also accept and
replace the exclamation mark
(!) with the same number in the
last digit so 1239! becomes
12399

34.5 Best Practices for Third Party PBX Integration
Successful third-party PBX integration with Deltapath UC platform means you can
simultaneously ring your Cisco desk phone and your Deltapath mobile and/or Skype for
Business client if you have purchased the Skype for Business Integration module on Deltapath.
▪ Cisco phone and any endpoints registered to Deltapath and/or Skype for Business
client ring at the same time with all incoming PSTN calls
▪ Cisco phone and any endpoints registered to Deltapath and Skype for Business client
ring at the same time with all incoming Skype for Business calls to the Cisco phone
extension.
▪ If you have Polycom phones registered directly to Deltapath UC platform and you dial
a Cisco extension, the expected behavior is for both the Cisco phone and Skype for
Business client to ring at the same time.
▪ If a Cisco phone dials a Polycom phone extension, the expected behavior is for both
the Polycom phone and Skype for business client to ring at the same time.
Integration Tips for Cisco Unified Communication Manager (CUCM)
The next section gives you a high-level understanding of how to integrate a third-party PBX
such as a Cisco Unified Communications Manager.

35.3 Configure Static or Dynamic Meeting Rooms
There are two ways to integrate with Polycom RMX. Both methods are described below.
• Option 1: Set Entry Queue Mode to Ad Hoc in RMX and route whatever range of room
numbers you desire from Deltapath UC platform through Outbound Routing. Use this
method when you do not have specific settings or reservation of resources for specific
conference room.
• Option 2: Create your desired conference rooms on the RMX and create Outbound
Routing that matches the conference room ranges created on RMX. Use this method
when you have different settings for different conference rooms such as participant
limits and reservation of resources.
Identify the type of conference rooms, dynamic or static, you want to create. The steps for
both are illustrated below.
Create Static Meeting rooms
1. Login to RMX Manager.
2. Click Frequently used in the left pane to view list.
3. Click Meeting Rooms.
4. Display Name: Enter the room owner’s name.

-- 338 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 338
5. ID: Enter a meeting room number.
Note: ID always refers to a meeting room number. It is important to ensure the assigned
meeting room number can be routed to the RMX. This is done in outbound routing where
you either identify the specific meeting room or identify a numbering pattern that
includes the ID in order to permit the room number to route to the RMX.
6. Conference Password: Optional Field. Enter a number if you want participants to enter a
password when calling to the RMX.
7. Chairperson Password: Optional Field. Enter a number if you want the meeting leader to
enter a password when calling to the RMX.
8. Reserve Resources for Video Participants: Click the up and down arrows to reserve spots
for video participants.
Note: This is an important step. RMX allows you to book resources. Since RMX defines the
maximum number of participants allowed to use the endpoint at any given time, you
never want to be in a situation where you invite participants to a meeting only to
discover they cannot be part of the meeting because the maximum number has been
reached due to others using the RMX in concurrent calls.
9. Reserve Resources for Voice Participants: Click the up and down arrows to reserve spots
for voice participants. See note in step 7.
10. Maximum Number of Participants: Enter the maximum number of participants permitted
in the meeting.
11. Click OK.

16. DTMF Mode: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click rfc2833. This is the recommended
default.
17. Copy Caller ID Name to Number: No
18. Global Conference Bridge Routing Method: This option does not apply if the Deltapath
UC platform field is set to No.
19. Authentication Mode: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click IP and Source Port.
20. Allow SIP Info Method: Enable (must be enabled for video to work properly with RMX)
21. Force RTP packets Through Server: Yes
22. Session timer: Click Accept. RMX requires SIP Session Timer to be enabled.
23. Session Expires: Enter a number in seconds. By default, the session expires in 1800
seconds for RMX.
24. Click Save.
For further explanation of the steps above and for full explanation of the fields in the SIP
Trunk Settings, see Adding SIP Trunk.

Conference room numbers or a number pattern must be created in outbound routing so users
can connect to the RMX. If you have configured minimum and maximum digits for conference
passwords, ensure these lengths are reflected in your outbound routing configuration so the
assigned meeting room number can route to the RMX.
1. Click
2. Type Outbound Routing in the textbox.
3. Click Outbound Routing.
4. Click Add Outbound Routing.

5. Number: This is the meeting room number range. Consult the Number Patterns table

6. Context: Click the dropdown arrow and then select a context. Recommended is Internal

36 Customized Databases
This section of the guide is for customers who have purchased the Intelligent IVR module and
who are using MYSQL. From a database perspective, the Deltapath UC application currently
only supports MySQL. The module allows you and your support team to access your self-
defined databases and perform management operations and operational maintenance on your
databases directly from the Deltapath Switchboard. In addition, self-defined databases are
integrated into the Interactive voice response (IVR) system allowing customers yet another
way to easily access and manage their databases. With IVR integration, customers can read
records, create records, update records, and delete records right from an IVR system.
You have the option of using an existing database if you have one already created. The
Customized Database utility will access the database and all the tables and records inside it.
Alternatively, you can create a new database and add tables and records right from the

-- 343 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0

For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 343
Deltapath Switchboard. All examples used in this section are simplified to make
understanding easier.
36.1 Setup MySQL Connection to Deltapath Switchboard
Local Database
It is important to set up your databased connection. If you are establishing a local database
connection, 3 key pieces of information are necessary:
• Database Location: Local databases are managed by Deltapath Switchboard
• Database Name: Identify the database that will be used when connected to the MySQL
server.
• Field Data Type: Define the maximum length of a string for your table columns.
Columns data type will be set to what is defined unless another data type is detected.
Possible values include:
o 128 characters (VARCHAR 128)
o 255 characters (VARCHAR 255)
o 65536 characters (TEXT)
You decide what data type fits your database needs.
Use the smallest length of a string for your application. Using the longest
length without justification or need will result in unnecessary degradation of
your table’s performance overall.
Example: If the maximum length of your data will be 200 characters, then use 255 characters
(VARCHAR (255)). Similarly, if you are uncertain how your table will be used in the future, it
may be best to use 65536 characters (TEXT).
Establish a Local Database Connection for an Existing Database
If your existing database is managed by Deltapath Switchboard, choose local database. All
databases created will have the prefix, customize_db_, automatically added to the
database’s name so Deltapath Switchboard can identify customer created databases.
1. Click
2. Type Customized Database Settings in the textbox.
3. Click Customized Database Settings.
4. Database Location: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click Use Local Database
5. Type: MySQL
6. Database Name: Click Choose Database from List to access a dropdown list containing the
names of existing databases.
7. Click the name of the database that will be used when connected to your MySQL server.
8. Field Data Type: Click the dropdown arrow and then click the most suitable field data
type.
9. Click OK. A Notice opens letting you know your database was created successfully.
10. Click OK.

Remote Database
If you are establishing a remote database connection, 6 key pieces of information are necessary:
• Database Location: Remote database
• Address: URL or IP address to your MySQL server.
• Username: Username that is used to access the database
• Password: Password associated with the username.
Note: The username and password are created by you. It is used in this dialog box to grant rights to
Deltapath UC to access your database. The username and password must have sufficient rights in
order to perform a number of database operations.
• Database Name: Identify the database that will be used when connected to the MySQL server.
Note: Certain names are restricted. You would need to select another name if the name created
uses restricted names such as Deltapath, crm, or ejabberd.
• Field Data Type: Define the maximum length of a string for your table columns. Columns data type
will be set to what is defined unless another data type is detected. Possible values include:
o 128 characters (VARCHAR (128))
o 255 characters (VARCHAR (255))
o 65536 characters (TEXT)
You decide what data type fits your database needs.
The length of the string you choose for your application should match your needs. Using the longest
length without justification or need will result in unnecessary degradation of your table’s
performance overall.
Example: If the maximum length of your data will be 200 characters, then use 255 characters (VARCHAR
(255)). Similarly, if you are uncertain how your table will be used in the future, it may be best to use 65536
characters (TEXT).

38 Data Type Detection
Anytime you create a table, the system inspects the data in the table and tries to determine
the data type that best fits each column. The system can only accurately determine a
column’s data type if all the values in every cell under the column satisfy all the criteria for a
data type. If no value has been introduced in the cells of the table, all columns will use the
Default Data Type defined in the Customized Database Settings dialog box, except the
_Deltapath-generated_id column.

-- 350 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 350
The following is a list of datatypes available and their Criteria:
Datatypes Criteria
Integer • Cell contains only integer values
Date/Time • When using Excel, the cell must have the date cell format
• The format must be Y-m-d or Y-m-d H:i:s
• Example: 2017-07-04 12:30:00
String • Any column that does not match integer or date/time data types is
identified as the default data type defined in the Customized
Database Settings dialog box.
Table 60: Datatypes
Examine the table below for a closer look at how data type detection works.
Based on the data in the table above, the following can be determined:
Columns Data Type
A • INT (Integer) will be assigned to the ticketid column
• Every cell in the column contains digits
B • The default data type, VARCHAR (255) will be assigned to
the show column
• While cell B2 is a string, the cell below it contains a
different format, digits only.
D • Date, will be assigned to the created_at column
• Every cell in the column has the Date cell format
• The format is Y-m-d

once changes are made.

will be generated automatically for the new record that was added. You can choose to assign
a value. However, this means you must ensure the value assigned is unique and therefore not
duplicated in any of the table’s records.

modified. In this step, the original table is wiped clean of all data and a new file is used to
repopulate the table with new data.

41 Voice Prompts
Voice prompts are needed in various areas of the switchboard. It is best practice to prerecord
your voice prompts, especially if you are creating an IVR system. Deltapath offers many
default system prompts if you do not want to record your own prompt.
To assist you, many of the voice prompts and the places you will need them when setting up a
contact center and an IVR are identified below. It is also critical to reference the section

-- 358 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 358
titled, Avoid Voice Prompt Mistakes in your Deltapath UC platform Administrator Guide.
Referencing this section will ensure your voice prompts are error free and professional.
• Interactive Voice Response (IVR) system voice prompts
For more information, see Interactive Voice Response.
o IVR Menu prompts
▪ Main menu introduction: Example – Welcome to Abbots college. Press 1 for
the operator, press 2 for Admissions, and Press 3 for Student Resources
▪ Caller entered an invalid option: Example – You made an invalid selection,
please try again.
▪ Caller did not select an option: Example - Your selection was not received,
please enter your selection now.
▪ Request caller to try again: Example - Please try again.
▪ Exceeded the number of retrial (invalid input): Example - You have
exceeded the number of attempts allowed to make a correct selection.
▪ Exceeded the number of retrials (no input): Example – You have not made
a selection and have exceeded the number of attempts allowed.
▪ Unavailable greeting: Example – I am currently unavailable. Please leave a
message and I will return your call.
▪ Busy Greeting: Example – I am currently busy. Please leave a message.
▪ User Entered an Invalid Option: If system tells caller to enter 1 or 2 and
the caller enters 3 then the caller is notified that the selection was invalid.
Caller will hear something like, you made an invalid selection, please try
again. You can also use the default option: error-invalid-option.
▪ User did not enter anything: Caller did not enter anything when requested
by the IVR system. Example: Your selection was not received, please enter
your selection now. You can also use the default option: error – no – input.
▪ Request user to try again: Callers are requested to please try again, when
they forget to select or when they make an invalid entry. The default
option can be used: error-pls-try-again
▪ Before Exit: Callers hear a final voice prompt before exiting the system.
Example: Thank you for your business. Have a great day.

was entered, please enter one now.

contacting Abbots College.

41.1 Avoid Voice Prompt Mistakes
Ensure your voice prompts are not full of problems and errors. Unprofessional sounding voice
prompts can negatively impact caller experience. To create seamless, unified, and
professional quality voice prompts throughout your IVR system, read the information below:
1. Too Many Menu Items: As a rule, 3-5 items should exist for each menu level. Any more
than 3-5 options can confuse callers prompting them to replay menu a prompt repeatedly.
This situation can be very frustrating for callers. Some callers may even hang up.
2. Not Enough Menu Items: If you do not give callers enough menu options, chances are they
will not be able to service their own inquiry and will need to be routed to a user. Equally
important, callers may be routed to the wrong user because not enough menu options
were offered.
3. Neglecting to Offer an Exit Option: It is recommended that callers have a number to
press to immediately exit the system and speak to a user during business hours. You may
also want to give callers the option to exit the IVR system and leave a voicemail.
4. Use of Unprofessional Prompts: Your IVR system is the first stop where callers have an
opportunity to formulate an impression of your organization. As a result, ensure your
voice prompts are clear, friendly, and professional.
5. After Hour Prompts: Callers need to always hear the correct prompts. Let callers know
there is no one available to take their calls after hours and they can call back during
normal business hours or leave a message. The prompt can also include emergency
contact numbers, or it can redirect callers to the IVR main menu.
6. Place frequently requested options at the beginning of the menu: Why have callers
wait to hear the most frequently requested options at the end of a menu? Aim to deliver
what your callers are looking for first, whenever it is possible.
7. Lack of Consistency: Announce prompts in a predictable pattern. For example: Do not say
Press 1 to reach the admissions office, press 2 to reach the undergraduate office, and
then for the post-Graduate office, Press 3. Be aware of what you are going to say, by
using the scripting area in the voice prompt dialog box.
In addition, employ a single voice for all menu prompts. Do not use an automated voice
response prompt halfway through your system and then switch to a customized voice. Aim
to keep things consistent.
41.2 Add Voice Prompts
All voice prompts are created from the Voice Prompt Settings dialog box. There are two ways
to add voice prompts. Follow the steps below to create your voice prompts.
Note: Acceptable file format for voice prompts includes MP3 and WAV files, but cannot
exceed 16 bits.

42 Interactive Voice Response System (IVR)
Once you have created your voice prompts, it is time to start building your IVR system. An IVR
system allows callers to interact with a company through a telephone keypad. Through an
IVR, callers can either service their own inquiries or they can be routed to a user group for
example in a specific department or an agent in a call center that can best service their
inquiries. All the IVR menus you create contain menu options and a voice prompt. There are 4
steps to creating an IVR system:
42.1 Create IVR menus
An IVR menu is the building block of an IVR. An IVR is often referred to as a tree, and the IVR
Menu as a building node in the tree. Each IVR menu can have many IVR options. Callers use a
dial pad to select IVR options.
Before you begin creating your IVR menu, ensure you understand the call flow and options you
need to create your multi-level IVR. Create a draft of your IVR system before you begin
creating menus so you understand how many menus you require, each menu level/tier, and
the options that must be associated with each menu. Below is a high-level example, which
shows the beginnings of an IVR tree.
Create IVR
menus
Add Voice
prompts to
menus
Create Menu
Options
Create IVR
phone number
to link callers to
the IVR system

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 366
Figure 21: Example of IVR Tree
To create a menu, follow the steps below.
1. Click
2. Type IVR.
3. Click IVR.
4. Click Add IVR. The IVR Menu dialog box opens.
Depending on your server version, you will see one of the following screens

-- 367 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0

Or

-- 368 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 368
5. Title: Enter the name of the menu that will be associated with one of the recorded voice
prompts.
Create a descriptive title as there are multiple menu levels in an IVR. It is
recommended you place a number at the end of the menu’s name to identify
the level of the menu, which will aid you in building and organizing the many
tiers of your IVR system. Example: Abbots College Main Welcome 1 and Abbots
Admissions 2. The number 1 next to Abbots College Main Welcome means that
this is a tier one menu. Similarly, Abbots Admissions is a tier 2 menu.
6. Customer: Click the dropdown and then Click the customer.
7. Access Right: Select the checkbox next to Access Right to Deny callers from dialing any
number they want in this system. For information on toll fraud, see Contexts and
Permission Groups.
Select the checkbox next to Access Right to ensure callers cannot dial any
number that might be associated to their permission group. If this box is
unchecked, callers can dial a number outside of the IVR options provided to
them and be routed to a real extension that you do not want them to gain
access to from the IVR. For example, if a caller was given the IVR options 1, 2,
or 3, but presses 5, the caller can be directed to extension 5 if it exists.
Alternatively, if you want to include an IVR option that informs callers they can
dial staff directly if they know their extension number, then the checkbox
would remain unchecked.
8. Skip Waiting for User Input: Select if you do not want the IVR to wait for the caller to
select an IVR option, but to instead go to the Action Before Exit and execute a
predefined operation.
When callers reach an IVR menu, the system typically waits for callers to
input a number. For example, a caller may be prompted to enter 1 for English
or 2 for Cantonese. However, an IVR can be setup to execute steps without
waiting for callers to enter a number. If you don’t want to configure the IVR
to wait for the caller to select an option, deselect the checkbox. As a result,
you will not need to configure Timeout for Waiting for User Input or voice
prompts.
9. Number of Times to Play this Menu: Enter a number. The number you enter determines
the number of times the voice prompt in this menu will be repeated before an exit option
is executed such as disconnecting the call. Example: If you enter the number 3, the voice
prompt plays three times if the caller does not enter an option or enters an invalid option
on the keypad. To skip directly to the Action before Exit, set the value to 1.
10. Timeout for Waiting User Input: Input the amount of time in seconds the system will wait
for callers to make their first selection on their keypad. This option is linked to the

-- 369 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 369
number you entered in the Number of Times to Play this Menu field. Example: If 10
seconds is entered. The system will wait for ten seconds for the caller to make a selection
then it repeats the menu. The system will do this three time before an exist option is
executed. To skip directly to the Action before Exit, set the value to 0.
11. Timeout for Entering User Input: The number you enter in seconds identifies the length
of time the system will wait in between each number you need to enter. Example: You
entered 3 seconds. Callers must enter extension 1027. Enter 1 – you have 3 seconds to
enter 0. Enter 0 – you have 3 seconds to enter 2. Enter 2 – You have 3 seconds to enter 7.
If you don’t enter the second or third number within the 3 second timeline, then the
system will consider the caller has finished. To skip directly to the Action before Exit, set
the value to 0.
Note: If callers are only required to enter one digit, then enter zero in this field.
42.2 Prompts
For each voice prompt below, Click the dropdown arrow and then Click the voice prompt that
was recorded or use a default prompt where available. Although not advisable, it is important
to note that you do not need to assign a voice prompt to all or any prompts. Empty prompts
will not be used and therefore, nothing is played.
12. Voice Menu Introduction: This voice prompt is typically a welcome message with menu
options, and the first thing callers hear when they reach your IVR system.
Note: All the voice prompts you recorded are conveniently located in the dropdown list.
13. User Entered an Invalid Option: This prompt is played when a caller selects an option
that is not defined. If the system tells the caller to enter 1 or 2 and the caller enters 3
then the caller is notified that the selection was invalid. The caller hears something like,
you made an invalid selection, please try again. Select the default option: error-invalid-
option or record your own voice prompt.
Note: If callers exceed the number of attempts allowed, they will no longer hear this
message. Instead, callers hear the Exceeded the number of retrial (invalid input) prompt.
14. User did not enter anything: Caller did not select an option when requested by the IVR
system. Example: Your selection was not received, please enter your selection now.
Select the default option: error – no – input or record your own voice prompt.
Note: If callers exceed the number of attempts allowed, they will no longer hear this
message. Instead, callers hear the Exceeded the number of retrial (no input) prompt.
15. Request user to try again: Callers are requested to please try again when they forget to
make a selection or when they make an invalid entry. The default option can be used:
error-pls-try-again.

-- 370 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 370
16. Exceeded the number of retrial (invalid input): Caller repeatedly makes invalid
selections, and the caller exceeds the number of attempts allowed. Select the default
option: error-invalid-exceeded or record your own voice prompt.
17. Exceeded the number of retrials (no input): Callers are informed the system has not
received a selection and they have exceeded the number of attempts allowed. Select the
default option: error-noinput-exceeded or record your own voice prompt.
18. Before Exit: Callers hear a final voice prompt before exiting the system. Example: Thank
you for your business. Have a great day.
42.3 Action Before Exit
Select a step you want executed. These steps do not require the caller to choose an IVR
option. The screenshot below illustrates all of the options available under Action Before
Exit. Each one is discussed in detail.
Action Before Exit can be especially useful for callers that have trouble navigating or
using your IVR. Since callers should never be disconnected prematurely, you can offer
callers other options that are executed by the system.
19. Select an Action Before Exit option.

For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 371
General Actions
Set Language
When selected, all voice prompts that are played, including prompts in the IVR menu, will use
the language you select in the dropdown list.
If your voice prompts are recorded in different languages such as English and Cantonese and
you choose to Set Language to Cantonese, then the Cantonese prompts will be played. See
Add Voice Prompts for more information.
Playback Voice Prompt
Select a voice prompt to play from a dropdown list. If you did not specify a language under
Set Language, the default language used in all voice prompts is English regardless of whether
you recorded your voice prompts in different languages.
Read User Input
Variables are used to store information that is referenced and manipulated in the system. A
variable also provides a way of labeling data with a descriptive name. In Read User Input, the
information inputted by a caller is read and placed into a variable. The rest of this section
identifies the steps under Read User Input.

-- 372 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 372
1. Variable to Store the User Input: Enter a name for the variable that will store the
information provided by a caller. Variables can contain Uppercase alphabets (A-Z), digits
(0-9), and underscore (_). For date and time, the system will allow you to use special
format codes. See Say Time for more information on these codes.
A variable can store any value at all. Values can be upper or lowercase alphabets and
digits. It can contain a space, period (.), colon (:), and dash (-).
Example: FIRST_NAME= Calvin
In the example above, Calvin is the value that is stored in the variable, FIRST_NAME. Since
the string, Calvin is stored in the variable, you only need to reference the variable in the
system when required.
2. Prompt Before Enter Input: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click the voice prompt
that will play before a caller provides any input. Ideally, the prompt should inform callers
what they need to enter.
3. Number of Retrial(s) Allowed: Enter the number of chances a caller will be given to
provide the requested information.
4. Input Timeout (second): Enter a number. This number represents the amount of time in
seconds that a caller is given to begin entering the requested information.
5. Prompt when Exceed Input timeout: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click a voice
prompt that informs callers they did not provide input in time. Example: I’m sorry, we did
not receive your information, please input it now.
6. Prompt when Exceed Number of Retrial: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click a
prompt that informs callers the system has not received the required input from them and
they have exceeded the number of attempts allowed.
7. Prompt Before Call Disconnect: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click a voice prompt
that will play right before the call is disconnected. Example: Thank you for calling. If you
are experiencing difficulties call Support at 1-800-250-2500.

-- 373 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 373
8. Action when Exceed Number of Retrial: This is the action the system will take when a
caller has exceeded the number of chances given to provide the requested information.
The best practice is to disconnect the call. Also note that this is not a configurable option.
To better understand the information explained in this section, consider a caller who wants to
check his account balance using a telephone banking hotline. The telephone banking hotline
will use Read User Input to gather information about the caller’s bank account number.

Ascending Sorts Descending Sorts
• Text: A to Z - for text data
• Number: Smallest to Largest
• Date or time: Oldest to
Newest
• Text: Z to A- for text data
• Number: Largest to Smallest
• Date and Time: Newest to
Oldest

42.3.7.3 Download Voice Recordings
You can download IVR voice recordings.

2. Follow the download prompts to open the recording.
Download Multiple Voice Recordings:
1. Select the checkboxes next to all the voice recording records you want to download.
2. Follow the download prompts to open the recordings.
42.3.7.4 Delete Voice Recordings
Any voice recording that is deleted is permanent. You cannot retrieve these recordings.
Delete One Voice Recording:
1. Click the X to the right of the voice recording record you want to delete.
2. Click Yes to remove the voice recording. The record is deleted from the system.
Delete Multiple Voice Recordings:
3. Select the checkboxes next to all the voice recording records you want to delete.
4. Click Manage Selected Records at the top of the IVR Voice Recording dialog box.
5. Click Yes to remove all the recordings selected. The voice recordings are deleted.
Disconnect Call
The system disconnects the caller.
It is best practice not to make this choice as there are many alternatives. For example,
send callers to voicemail or to the extension number of another person.

➢ No Greeting: Callers do not hear a greeting.
➢ Unavailable Greeting: Callers hear a prompt that announces the other party they are
trying to reach is unavailable.
➢ Busy Greeting: Callers hear a prompt that announces the party is busy at the moment.

Route to Queue
Route callers to a queue.

queue before timing out and returning callers to the IVR.
What happens when the routing attempt times out depends on the setup of the IVR tree. The
IVR may be set up to return callers to the previous IVR menu or Forward callers to voicemail
and play a prompt that tells callers “All agents are busy. Please leave a message and your call
will be returned during the next business day.”
Enter a large enough number so callers can wait for their calls to be
answered for as long as they want. If the call gets disconnected it should be
because the caller decided to hang up and not because the system
disconnected the caller.

Customized Database
A client in this section means a company using the services of another company such as
Deltapath. It is not to be confused with Customer, which in this guide means a person that
buys goods or buys or uses services from a company.
The customized database options available in the IVR makes it simple for clients to conduct
common database operations and manage their database records fast and conveniently using
an IVR. Specifically, clients can read, create, update, and delete records using the IVR.
Just imagine the possibilities. An employee can help a customer register an account by using
the create record option. Perhaps a customer has changed his address. The Update record
option can be used to edit the customer’s existing record. One of the simplest, but highly
relevant examples is the need for the client to delete old or irrelevant records to reduce
table size or improve database performance. The Delete record option in the IVR can be used.
Read Records
An example will be used to explain this section.
A customer calls the telephone banking hotline, which has an option that allows
customers to verify their most recent transaction record. For example, the bank customer
is interested in finding out if his paycheck was deposited.
One can safely assume at this point that the bank already has the customer’s account
number stored in a variable like “BANKACCT”. The transaction records for this account
need to be retrieved from the database. The Read Records step is needed to read the
transaction record to the customer. The steps are discussed below to illustrate this
example:

Extended Discussion
Based on the table in this example, there are 5 records associated to account 1000. This
means there are multiple records matching your conditions. This presents an issue as only the
first record will be read based on when you select Read Records. Based on the screenshot
below, the highlighted record will be read.
This record is not what the customer is expecting. It is dated 2017-03-01 and therefore, it is
NOT the latest record. To fix this situation, you can manipulate the order records are listed in
when conditions are matched.
Under Action do the following:
6. Order by: Select the field. In this example, it is operated_at.
7. Click Ascending or Descending. In this example, Descending order is selected so the
most recent records appear at the top of the records list.
What we just did is to enforce the ordering. We order by “operated_at” in descending order
so the latest one becomes the first record. It is just the same as you go to the Customized
Database Table Record Viewer and do a “Sort Descending” on the “operated_at” column

Create Record
Use the IVR to insert a record into a table in your customized database. The transaction table

variables in the dialog box.

o Specify the values that will be inserted into each column in the table

o Possible values:
▪ Customized Value
▪ Specify any value to be inserted
▪ Variable
▪ Insert the value of a variable into the column
▪ Current Date and Time
▪ Insert the current Date and Time (at the time of execution)
▪ The format will be Y-m-d H:i:s, e.g. 2016-08-12 12:30:00
▪ The column must have a data type of Date or string format with sufficient
string length restriction
▪ Empty Value (NULL)
▪ Insert a NULL value
▪ Important Note: If the column is an auto_increment id, for example the
_Deltapath_generated_id in our Customized Database, you must use this
option to ensure the id are assigned correctly
In the example being used, a new function has been added to the telephone banking hotline.

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 391
The next section puts into perspective what the values inserted mean or how they are used
• _Deltapath_generated_id: This is a system-generated field that will apply a unique
number to every transaction. It is left empty because it is a database requirement. If
you want the number to auto increment, the value must be empty (Empty Value
(Null)).
• account: The account number is from a variable. The telephone banking hotline
should have asked the customer for his account number.
• operation: The operation is a customized value meaning the input can be anything.
The newly introduced operation “pay_credit_card” is used to define the operation
• amount: This is a variable. This can be a value read from the Credit Card database to,
so a customer knows how much is owed on the credit card.
• operated_at: Current Date and Time are used. When a credit card payment is made is
important information for customers and the bank. Specifically, this is the time when
the account owner calls the telephone banking hotline and pays with credit card. The
IVR inserts the time when the IVR executes the payments.
Note: The table field must be set to DATETIME to make things work correctly.

Update Records
Update an existing record by changing a value.
If the conditions setup identifies more than one record, then ALL records are updated.
Options
• Table
Select the table to find a record(s)
• Condition
▪ To update an existing record, you must first specify which record(s) you want to
update. To filter the records, use conditions
▪ Column
▪ Identify the field (column) to do the comparison
▪ Operator
▪ Specify the operator for doing a comparison. The operators are:
▪ Equals to
▪ The column’s value is equal to the value specified
▪ Greater than
▪ The column’s value is greater than the value specified
▪ Less than
▪ The column’s value is less than the value specified
▪ Contains
▪ Any part of the column’s value is matching the value specified
▪ Begins with
▪ The column’s value begins with the value specified
▪ Ends with
▪ The column’s value ends with the value specified
▪ Matches Regular Expression
▪ The column’s value satisfies the regular expression specified
▪ The regular expression *DOES NOT NEED to have beginning and ending
delimiters, i.e. [a-z0-9]
▪ Use regular expression to match a string. Example: “Calvin Lau” Equals “Calvin
Lau”. Here you are doing an exact match. If there is an “Eric Lau” then there

-- 393 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 393
will be no match. With regular expression, you can state, “Calvin Lau” matches
regular expression “^. * LauL$” . The “^.* Lau$” is a pattern that says that will
not only match “Calvin Lau”, but it will also match “Eric Lau” and “Daniel
Lau.” Note: This is only one simple use for Regular Expression.
▪ Is Empty
▪ The column’s value is Null or is an empty string
▪ Remove Condition
▪ Remove the current condition
▪ There must be at least one condition available
▪ Add Condition
▪ Add more criteria to filter the records
• Column Value Update
▪ Specify how each column should be updated, and update to what value
▪ You must specify what value to be updated into ALL columns, though for those
unneeded columns you can still use “keep Original Value” or “Empty Value”
▪ Possible values:
▪ Keep Original Value
▪ Keep the original value of the column in that particular record
▪ Note: If the column is an auto_increment id, for example the
_Deltapath_generated_id, usually it is best practice to maintain this id. In this
case always use “Keep Original Value” and NOT NULL
▪ Customized Value
▪ You can specify any value to be inserted
▪ Variable

▪ Insert the value of a variable into the column
▪ Current Date and Time
▪ Insert the current Date and Time (at the time of execution)
▪ The format will be Y-m-d H: i:s, e.g., 2016-08-12 12:30:00
▪ The column must have a data type of Date or string format with sufficient

Delete Records
Delete existing records from a table.
You cannot revert to the original state of a table. Once records are deleted, they are
gone. Similarly, if the conditions you use to match records are too wide and more than
one record is returned, it is important to know that all the recorded matches will be
deleted when this step is executed.
