Conditional Routing
Callers are routed to an IVR Menu if the condition defined is satisfied. A condition is an
expression that dictates an outcome or desired state. Condition settings are based on
properties, comparative operators, and values such as a string, Equal, or Not Equal
1. Check If: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click one of the following subjects:
• Current Date (YYY-MM-DD): The date the IVR is called. Example: 2016-08-12
• Current Time (24-hour format): The time the IVR is called. Example: 12:30:00
• Current Day of Week: The day of the week the condition is executed is deemed the
current week.
• Current Unix Timestamp: The timestamp when the condition is executed.
• Custom Variable: Custom Variable is discussed below.
2. Operator: Click the dropdown arrow and click an option that will define the relationship
between the subject you defined in step 1 with the value you define in step 3
Note: All options in the dropdown list are defined below. However, the option you have
access to in step 2 depends on the subject you choose in step 1.
• Equals To: Check if the subject defined in step 1 is equal to the value defined in step
3.
• Not Equals to: Check if the subject defined in step 1 is not equal to the value defined
in step 3.

-- 400 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 400
• Greater Than: Check if the subject defined in step 1 is greater than the value defined
in step 3.
• Less Than: Check if the subject defined in step 1 is less than the value defined in step
3.
• Within (Date): The Within (Date) operator ensures the subject in step 1, which is a
Date Time value, occurs within a specific period of time. The subject must follow the
format: YYYY-MM-DD HH: ii:ss such as 2017-04-18 12:30:00.
Note: If the subject is Current Date (YYY-MM-DD) and the operator is Within (date),
only the date is compared.
• Before (Date and Time): The Before (Date) operator ensures the subject in step 1,
which is a Date Time value, occurs before the date identified in step 3.
• After (Date and Time): The After (Date) operator ensures the subject in step 1, which
is a Date time value, occurs after the date identified in step 3.
• Has String Length Equals to: Check if the string length of the subject defined in step
1 is equal to the string length defined in step 3.
• Matches Regular Expression: This operator allows you flexibility when matching a
string. Here is a simple example of how to use this option: “Calvin Lau” Matches
Regular Expression “^.* Lau$”
The “^.* Lau$” is a regular expression pattern. This pattern indicates that you are
not only looking to match “Calvin Lau.” As a result, Eric Lau and Tanya Lau are
returned as a match as well.
Note: Regular expressions must have beginning and ending delimiters.
Example: /[a-z0-9]/
3. Values: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click a value.
• Today: The subject in step 1 must be within, before, or after the current date
when the IVR is called.
• This Month: The subject in step 1 must be within/before/after the current month
when the IVR is called.
• This Year: The subject in step 1 must be within/before/after the current year
when the IVR is called.
• Customized Value: The subject in step 1 must be within/before/after the date
represented by the value. The value must be in the following format: YYYY-MM-DD
such as 2017-04-18.

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0

42.3.21.1 Common Practices in Date and Time Comparison
This section aims to provide you with more examples and information that will help you
understand and correctly use Date time comparisons.
In the dropdown list under Conditional Routing, there are four date options:
• Current Date (YYYY-MM-DD)
• Current Time (24-hour format)
• Current Day of Week
• Current Unix timestamp
Each one of these date options is discussed in greater details below.
Current Date
Check If Current date (YYY-MM-DD)
Operator Frequently Used:
• Within (Date)
• Before (Date and Time)
• After (date and Time)
Seldom Used:
• Equal to
Use Within (Date) instead
• Not Equal to
• Matches regular expression
Advance pattern matching seldom appears in date time
comparisons.
Values Frequently Used:
• Customized Value
The customized value should be in the same format as Current
date, YYYY-MM-DD
Seldom Used:
• Today
• This Month
• This Year

This section explains why using the Current date with frequently used operators and

1. When conditional routing is setup as depicted in the screenshot below, the condition is
always true. As such, there is no reason for you to initiate and verify this condition as the
results are always the same.
2. If you setup Conditional Routing as shown in the two screenshots below, it will be
impossible for these conditions to ever be true. As a result, you should never explore a
relationship under these conditions.

44.1 Fax Number Mapping
Fax number mapping has two purposes. One, you can create fax numbers for users. Two, this
number is presented as your outbound caller ID when users send faxes.
Without Fax Number Mapping you cannot create a fax number for users and their personal
extensions would show up as the caller ID. Faxes sent to their personal numbers would fail.

fax number.
7. Click OK.
8. Click OK.

fax number.

45 Multitenant Configuration
Multi-tenant configuration allows a single cluster to service various customers or tenants as
they are often referred to, by providing a dedicated share of the instance to each customer
through virtual partitions, which promotes customer privacy and confidentiality while
providing increased Return on Investments (ROI) for service providers. For example, telecom
or cloud service providers offer services to a large number of customers residing on the same
system, taking advantage of the multi-tenant configuration.
Objectives of multitenant configurations:
▪ Companies cannot dial the extensions of other companies
▪ Companies never see each other in the site phonebook
▪ Billing is kept separately for each company
Group and Customer are Equal to Company
Throughout the configuration windows in Deltapath, Group or Customer
information is required. In a multi-tenant configuration, both Group and
Customer is synonymous with company. As a result, all users in a group or users
that belong to a customer also belong to the same company. This means all
related configurations such as Fax to email numbers, contact center agents,
contact center queue, users, and the IVR menu must be bound to a group or
customer and that group or customer must be bound to a specific company.

-- 423 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 423

How to Deal with Extension Conflicts
In a multi-tenancy environment, you may encounter different clients hosting on the same
platform who would like to have the same range of extensions, which can cause conflict when
creating the user in Deltapath. To avoid user extension conflict from arising, use the full DID
number as the user extension then also create a number _ZXXX within the tenant extension
context.
Example:
Abbots DID range 34561000 to 34561099
Abbots extension range 1000 to 1099
Brookes DID range 65431000 to 65431099
Brookes extension range 1000 to 1099
In the above example, both tenants would like to have the same extension range 1000 to
1099; however, since a user’s extension is a unique field in Deltapath, you cannot define the
same number in the same Deltapath UC. To successfully handle this situation, do the
following:
• Use the full number in the DID range as the user extension.
• Create a number _ZXXX for each of the tenants that handles 4-digit extension dialing
within the group using the redirect number function:
• Select Redirect Call and add “3456”, the DID prefix, and tie it to the same tenant
extension context Abbots Internal.

-- 428 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 428
• Configure the Caller ID so the call recipient will receive a 4-digit extension as a caller
ID, by
o selecting Rewrite under Caller ID Number
o entering 4 for example, as the Number of Digit To Strip.
Taking the steps above will allow your clients to have the same extension numbers without
any conflict.

45.6 Extension and Short Dial Assignment
In a multi-tenancy environment, you may encounter different clients hosting on the same
platform who would like to have the same range of extensions, which can cause conflict when
creating the user in Deltapath. To avoid user extension conflict from arising, use the E.164
format as the user extension and then create a number _ZXXX within the tenant extension
context.
E.164 is an international numbering plan where each assigned phone number contains the
following:

-- 440 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 440
• Country code (CC)
• National destination code (NDC)
• Subscriber number (SN)
There can be up to 15 digits in an E.164 number. By using the E.164 numbering format, each
phone number is guaranteed to be unique worldwide, preventing any possibility of number
clashing.
Example:
Abbots DID range 34561000 to 34561099
Abbots extension range 1000 to 1099
Brookes DID range 65431000 to 65431099
Brookes extension range 1000 to 1099
In the above example, both tenants would like to have the same extension range 1000 to
1099; however, since user extension is a unique field in Deltapath, you cannot define the
same number with the same Deltapath UC. To successfully handle this situation, do the
following:
• Use the full number in the DID range as the user extension.
• Create a number such as _ZXXX for each of the tenants to handle 4-digit extension
dialing within the group using the redirect number function:
Use E.164 Format when Creating Users.
1. Each user’s extension should follow the E.164 format.
In Deltapath, no two users can ever have the same extension even if both users
belong to a different customer or group.
For more information on how to complete the User Settings dialog box, See Users.
Creating a Short Dial Number
It is highly unlikely anyone wants to memorize or dial an assigned E.164 number. It is
recommended that a short dial number such as the last 3 or 4 digits of the phone number be
used. To do this, follow the high-level steps below:
1. Number: Enter a number in the Number dialog box under Number Settings.

46 Call Recording Management
This chapter introduces you to call recordings. The Call Recording module must be purchased
to have access to call recordings. Recording calls has many significant benefits and purposes.
Monitor call recordings to meet your service quality goals by rating customer satisfaction.
Sample calls to create quality coaching programs that focus on communication styles and
product knowledge. Or, manage compliance and corporate liability with recorded calls.

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 443
46.1 Four Ways to Enable Call Recording
Call recordings can be enabled in four different places in the Deltapath Switchboard.
Depending on the modules you have purchased, you may not have access to all the recording
options below.
1. Queue: All inbound calls to the queue are recorded when call recording is enabled at the
queue level.
2. Seat/Extension: Enable call recording for specific seats. For example, if you want to
record calls only at mobile desks then enable call recordings at the user level.
3. User: To record inbound calls and outbound calls, enable call recording at the user/seat
level. As a result, anyone that calls extension 1027 and any calls made from extension
1027 are recorded. It is important to note that enabling calls at this level means all calls,
inbound or outbound, made from the phone are recorded.
4. Number: When call recording is enabled at a phone number level, everything associated
with the number is recorded. For example, all calls are recorded for the queues linked to
the number. In addition, if the number reroutes callers to an outside number such as a
mobile phone, the call is recorded.
Note: A seat is also thought of as a user because they are essentially the same. For example:
Extension 1027 is not only registered to a specific phone, but also to a specific person. In
addition, if call recording is enabled on the server, RTP packets will always pass through the
server.
On Demand Recording: This feature is available if you purchased the recording module, and
you enable On Demand Recording in the ACL Permission Group. If enabled, users can record
calls whenever they need to by clicking the On-Demand button.
Queue:
1. Click
2. Type Queue in textbox.
3. Click Queue.
4. Select Enable next to Call Recording.

Adding Notes
After a call ends, users typically need a place to add additional details about the call such as
the next steps that must be taken, call resolution status, order details, or account
information. Supervisors reviewing users calls or following up with escalated call issues also
require a place to document details about a call. The Call Recording window is the place
where this all happens. Users can access their own recordings in the Call Recording window
and add notes after each call. Unlike users, supervisors may have access to all their teams
call recordings. Supervisors can add notes to any user’s call recording file.
To add notes after a call, here is what users and their supervisors will do:
1. To add notes, click Modify to the right of the call record. The Call Recording File opens.
A call recording cannot be modified. The modify feature allows users and supervisors
to add information under customized fields.
2. Enter notes in the boxes.
In the example below, the user has noted that the caller’s name is Sherry and that the call
was about a billing issue. In the summary, the user has not only identified the resolution but

Video recordings can drastically increase your storage consumption. The table below
communicates the file size consumed per minute based on call rate and resolution, which can
be used to help make critical decisions about video quality and storage space.

46.5 Identify the Perfect Call Recording Model and Archive Schedule
The number of hours you can record calls depends on the call recording model purchased. The
U32 hardware model is designed to hold 1500 hours of audio recording. The U256 holds 20,000
hours of audio recordings, and the U512 holds 30,000 hours of audio recordings.
Identifying the model you require, and how often you need to archive recordings is relatively
simple. Estimate the hours of recording required by determining the number of users and
their work hours. For example, if you have 35 users that work 8 hours each day, five days a
week that amounts to 1400 hours of recording each week. If you purchase the U32 model, you
will want to archive your recording every few days. Or you may decide to purchase the U256,
which will give you more archiving flexibility. As the number of users increases in the future,
you can upgrade to a different model.

archived files on your local server is highly recommended as it impacts overall

47.2 Deltapath Mobile Application
Mobile apps running in the background on a device can be suspended by the operating system
for several reasons. As a result, this unpredictable behavior can prevent users from receiving
calls and messages in Deltapath Mobile.
Deltapath Mobile requires the use of Apple Push Notification and Google Firebird Cloud
Messaging to ensure successful delivery of messages and calls. This means the server must
have outbound Internet connectivity. If general Internet is not permitted at your company, it
is recommended that you at least permit outbound connection to Apple and Google’s
network:
• Apple: 17.0. 0.0/8
• Google: Whitelist ports 5228-5230 outbound to any IP
If your organization must place a restriction to Google’s IP Block, ensure you have a way to
update the IP address frequently. Google advertises its IP block at the following URL in json
format: https://www.gstatic.com/ipranges/goog.json
Note, countries that block Google servers will affect Deltapath Mobile’s operation on
Android.

48 Instant Messaging
Deltapath UC has an Instant Message (IM) Module to embrace IM in the workplace and to
support different forms of communication. With Instant Messaging, users can connect to other
users inside their organization. IM is especially useful where real-time communication is
needed every day, but not necessarily spoken. Instant message comes with many features
including, but not limited to encrypted messages, group chat, voice and video capability, file
antivirus checker and delivery tracking.
By design, Instant Messaging is tied to an extension number, which is tied to a user. Users
with these standard user/extension accounts immediately have instant messaging once it is
configured. As a result, Instant Messaging users in Deltapath gain a unique user experience
compared to other free instant messaging tools available on the market. Unlike other
solutions, where IM is tied to a phone number with only a single device signed on, Deltapath
users may log into multiple devices including PC/web, or any iOS/Android devices. All device
messages are in sync, which means users do not have to worry about losing the device,
meaning losing all the chat log.
Call Center agents and administrators do not have traditional user accounts. As a result,
Instant Messaging is only available to these users when their accounts are configured so they
connect to a standard user account. This requires an additional step in ACL Users, which is
discussed later in this section. Once instant messaging is activated, agents and administrators
have instant messaging access via their standard user/extension account.
Note: Allow permission to Site phonebook. Users will be unable to look up and message
others in a one-to-one chat or group chat without it.

48.2 Instant Messaging Chat Log
The Instant Message Chat log chronicles chat conversations and keeps them on record. There
are two tabs: Chat, which involves one-to-one text chatting and Group Chat, which involves
group texts, especially useful for businesses. Both tabs function identically. Any file attached
during a one-to-one chat, or a group chat are displayed and can be downloaded or exported in
the Instant Messaging Chat Log.

Invite Someone was invited to a group chat
Kick A participant in a group chat was ejected from the group chat
Leave A participant voluntarily left the group chat
Title The name of the chat group has changed

51 Web Hook Management
A webhook is an event-driven communication between two application programming
interfaces (APIs). Deltapath implemented web hook events for incoming calls, voicemail, and
IM one-to-one and group chat. When a specific event occurs, the webhook pings the
customer’s application.
1. Click
2. Type Web Hook Management in the textbox.
3. Click Web Hook Management then click Add.
4. URL: Enter a URL.
5. Timeout: Enter a time in milliseconds.
6. Events: Select an event
7. Customer: Select the customer
8. Select Enabled and then click OK.
9. Click OK.
In the screenshot above, whenever a message is received from Comm100 users, a trigger is
sent to the URL defined.
52 Salesforce Integration
Deltapath delivers a robust experience with the integration of Salesforce including, but not
limited to the following:
• Deltapath users receive real time information from Salesforce about contacts,
opportunities, and cases on their switchboards when answering incoming calls.
• Users can lookup cases, opportunities, contacts, and accounts right from their
switchboard
• Log outgoing calls to Salesforce.com with the opportunity to add a detailed summary
of each call.
• Assigned sales tasks are displayed when users sign into Deltapath Switchboard and
Deltapath Mobile so users can call customers with a tap of the finger.

52.1 Prerequisites for Salesforce Integration with Deltapath
Salesforce integration with Deltapath only works with editions that have API access. These
editions include:
• Enterprise Edition
• Unlimited Edition
• Developer Edition
• Performance Edition
Note: The Professional Edition can be used if API access is purchased.
52.2 Salesforce Authentication Requirements
Salesforce requires five credentials for authentication. To identify a user’s account,
Salesforce requires the following:
• Username
• Password
• Security token
Salesforce must also identify the application attempting to access and use its database such
as Deltapath. Before access is granted, Salesforce requires the following credentials:
• Consumer key
• Consumer Secret

account as account setup is straight forward. Instead, this section discusses the parameters
that you will need to configure to get the integration to work.

52.7 Login Management
Login Management is an optional tool for administrators. Although users provide their own
credentials under Salesforce Login Settings, you can view and manage all Salesforce login
credentials entered by users. This means you can add, remove, and edit a user’s credentials.
If you add a user in the Login Management tool, ensure you choose the correct ACL
Permission group. If you are not careful when you select an ACL permission, a user
may be given administrator rights and involuntarily gain access to Salesforce
credentials for an entire company.

character sets are UTF-8 and ISO-8859-1. For example, encoding ISO-8859-1 represents all
Latin characters and is understood by virtually all receiving email reader software.
Encoding UTF0-8 (Unicode) can represent virtually all the characters in any language
around the world. Users who are using non-Latin data such as Euro symbols, Chinese,
Japanese, etc.) can use this encoding.

52.9 Number Matching Patterns
There is no standard currently in place for storing phone numbers. As such, companies use
different data structures. Calls received in Deltapath switchboard are stored as a collection of
digits without spaces and without formatting symbols like hyphens, brackets, or other symbols
(+). Example: 9057386242. Salesforce on the other hand has a different data structure for
saving phone numbers. Example: (905) 738-6242.

-- 489 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 489
To ensure business opportunities and other important information stored in Salesforce such as
contacts, accounts, and cases is communicated in real-time in Deltapath switchboard when
there’s an incoming call, the caller number must match a number stored in Salesforce. To
ensure phone numbers are successfully matched, Deltapath has introduced number matching
patterns to ensure the different data structures used in companies doesn’t hinder the
successful integration between the unified communication and customer relationship
management (CRM) solution.
To create a number matching pattern using custom settings, follow the steps below:
1. Click
2. Type Salesforce in the textbox.
3. Click Phone Number Matching Patterns. The dialog box opens.
4. Pattern Resolver: Enter the pattern you want resolved. Example: 9057386242.
5. Select Custom Settings.
6. Click Add.
7. Enter a pattern that corresponds to the phone number structure in Salesforce. Example:
XXX-XXX-XXXX. The pattern defined is displayed under Patterns Resolver using the number
string that was first entered, 9057386242, which now becomes 905-738-6242.
Note: Since Salesforce can have numerous number structures, you can repeat steps 6 and
7 until all patterns are added. All the number structures defined will now be sent to
Salesforce for matching.
8. Click Save.

-- 490 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 490
52.10 Number Matching Profile
Instead of using Custom Settings, you can also select Use Profile under Patterns Editor. Before
you can select Use Profile, a Number Matching Profile must be created. Profiles that are
created contain numerous number structures, which removes the need to repeatedly enter
the same number structures as in the case when using Custom Settings. A default number
patterns profile containing 10 of the most common number structures has been created for
your convenience. However, if you wish to create a new profile, follow the steps below.
1. Click
2. Type Salesforce in the textbox.
3. Click Phone Number Matching Patterns Profile.
4. Click Add Profile.
5. Name: Enter a profile name.
6. Description: This is an optional field that can be used to add more details.
7. Click Add.
8. Enter a number structure.
9. Repeat steps 7 and 8 until all number structures are added.
10. Click Save.
To ensure all the profiles created appear in the Use Profile dropdown list in the Phone
Number Matching Patterns dialog box, select the checkbox next to Name to select all profiles
or select individual profiles you want displayed in the dropdown list.

-- 491 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 491
All profiles selected now appear in the Use Profile dropdown list.
52.11 API Consumption
Each Salesforce subscription edition has different API limits. Refer to the Salesforce site
below for detailed information about the total API Request Limits based on calls in a 24-hour
period for each edition:
https://developer.salesforce.com/docs/atlas.en-
us.salesforce_app_limits_cheatsheet.meta/salesforce_app_limits_cheatsheet/salesforce_app
_limits_platform_api.htm
It is important to note that limits are not defined based on each user. As a result, if your
organization exceeds the API limit in a 24-hour period, all users can be blocked from making
API calls. If you exceed your limit, contact Salesforce.

53 Push-To-Talk
Push-to-Talk is another module that customers can purchase from Deltapath. It can be added
to applications like Deltapath Mobile and Acute. It is an upgrade to conventional two-way
radios that offer real time communication, but without the range limitations because users
experience it over Wi-Fi and 5G/LTE cellular data.

54 Alarm and Incident Dispatch System
The Alarm and Incident Dispatch System module is another one of Deltapath’s
offerings that is extremely useful to customers who are interested in impacting
productivity, employee response time, and employee accountability. The Alarm and
Incident Dispatch System integrates with third party systems to extend customers’
existing management and dispatch applications to increase employee productivity by
providing real time information alerts, improving resolution times, and time tracking
capabilities.
The Deltapath Alarm and Incident Dispatch System triggers alarms forwarded from the
third-party management and dispatch system via RESTful API. Once the system
receives the information, the Alarm and Incident Dispatch System automatically
dispatches the alarm to your app users via notification.
All alerts’ codes sent by a third party via API to a Deltapath UC server with the Alarm and
Incident Dispatch System module follow a similar format.
http://<serveripaddress>/RESTful/index.php/post/bms/alarm/trigger?msg=ALERTCODE%20
CATEGORY%20OPERATIONCODE%20ALARMCONFIGID%20ALARMDESCRIPTION
where
<serveripaddress> = IP address of Deltapath UC platform with Alarm and Incident
Dispatch System module
<BMSalertcode> = Alert Code defined from the third-party management and dispatch system
<BMSAlarmConfigID> = Alarm ID from the third-party management and dispatch system
<AlarmDescription> = Alarm Description from Alarm Settings

55 UMC Setup

1. Click
2. Type Numbering Plan.

4. Click Add a Number. The Number Settings dialog box opens.
5. Number: Enter the number users need to dial.
Note: This number can be anything such as *5, 10, or 1000.
If you have more than one Deltapath platform in your company in different
geographical locations, it is best practice to have the same number for all the UMCs
across your organization. This ensures the user experience is the same across
locations. For example, if John is accustomed to dialing *1 to access the UMC when he
is working from his California office, then he will expect when he travels to the Japan
office that he needs to dial *1 to access the UMC.

56 User Roaming
Some organizations have shared workspaces for mobile workers and guests. Workers need to
sign into a docking station through the UMC. The docking station is the physical phone on the
desk. When employees log into a phone at a workstation, their user settings and profile are
carried over such as their telephone number, phonebook, and caller ID. This workflow is
referred to by many names including hoteling, hot-desking, and user roaming.
All roaming users must sign into a docking station through the User Management Center
(UMC). Users must access the UMC and follow the prompts to do the following:
▪ Users can sign into the docking station.
▪ Users can log out of the docking station. The phone is rebooted after the user signs
out.
▪ Users can extend their docking station period from the UMC.
Once employees have finished using their temporary devices, they must sign out using the
UMC by following the voice prompts.
56.1 Identify a Phone as a Docking Station:
Follow the steps below to classify a phone as a docking station so that different users can sign
on.
1. Click
2. Type User.
3. Click User.
4. Click Add User or Click on Modify on the right-hand side of an existing user.
5. Scroll down to Equipment.

-- 506 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 506
6. Docking Station: Yes
7. Click OK.
The user/phone should be configured so no one can dial out from the PSTN on the
main extension of the desk phone, except an emergency number such as 911. If
the main extension has a DID mapped to it then users can receive and make calls,
which discourages users from signing in.
You can also identify a device as a docking station in Equipment.
1. Click
2. Type Equipment.
3. Click Equipment.
4. Locate the user in the Equipment list and then Click Modify Equipment
5. Docking Station: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click Yes.

The following features may not work as expected due to limitations of the Cisco Phone.
56.2 Remote Roaming and Docking Stations Setup on Cisco Phones
Cisco phones can only register to one server. As a result, when the roaming user’s number
moves over to the docking station on line one of the phones, the original line one account
automatically moves to line 2. The docking station will be unable to register to the original
server so line 2 will not be operational unless it happens to be registered to the same server
the user is signed into. In this situation, both lines can coexist without issues.
56.3 Set Docking Station User Roaming Expiration
Often times, users forget to sign out. As a best practice, administrators should configure user
roaming to expire at a given time. As a result, users who forget to sign out, eventually get
logged off. To set up an expiry time, follow the steps below.
1. Click
2. Type General Settings.
3. Click General Settings.

57 DISA
DISA, also known as remote access, allows employees to leverage their company’s PBX to
make work related calls that may incur call related charges. The use of a DISA number is
especially useful for employees that do not have Deltapath Mobile internet access, and need
to use their own personal phones to make a call. For example, employees using their own
personal phones can call their company’s PBX. Once connected, they can make an
international call. The long-distance costs are always billed under the caller’s account in the
company and never to the caller’s personal number. In addition, the caller’s number and
caller ID are always protected as the destination number seen and/or Caller ID belongs to the
company.
To define a DISA number, follow the instructions below.
1. Click
2. Type System Number.
3. Click System Number. The System Number dialog box opens.
4. DISA (Remote Access) Service Number: Enter a number.
The DISA (Remote Access) Service Number you enter must conform to the internal extension
range defined in Inbound routing.

58 Virtual Meeting
Virtual Meeting is a scheduling tool that gets attendees to their meeting destination and
should not be confused with a conference bridge. Virtual Meeting is a gateway that directs
meeting attendees to all SIP based meetings. For example, virtual meeting directs attendees
to the Deltapath UC platform internal audio conference bridge, Polycom RealPresence
Collaboration Server (RMX), Dolby Conference phone, your meeting rooms running HDX Series
or RealPresence Group Series, RealPresence Trio. When using IP, meeting attendees may use
any SIP based endpoints and enjoy toll-free conferencing. In addition, Virtual Meeting extends
meetings to Skype for Business client, Consumer Skype, and adds WebRTC browser call-in
capability.
Audio Conference Bridge Security
Virtual Meeting is also designed to offer organizations extra security. Access information
conveyed to attendees such as access codes can only be used during a specific date and time
unlike the passcodes communicated for a conference bridge. With a conference bridge,
someone is required to change the conference PIN regularly and promptly to ensure past
conference attendees and other uninvited callers cannot access the same conference room at
unexpected times. If you want to keep your conference room private and reduce the need

-- 512 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 512
for someone to regularly change conference PIN, then virtual meeting may be right for your
organization.
Video Endpoints Security
Extra security is also offered to all your video endpoints such as HDX series and Group series
to ensure no one ever has open access to any of your video conference equipment. Once
attendees access rights expire, they cannot use their access information anymore such as
Enabling External Dial-in via SIP
External participants may use any SIP equipment and dial-in with a SIP URL such as
sip:$accesscode@sip.deltapath.com to enjoy HD voice, avoid any toll, and HD video.
58.1 Virtual Meeting Email Templates
There are many email templates available for virtual meetings such as Invite Virtual meeting
attendee, Update Virtual Meeting attendee, and Cancel Virtual Meeting Attendee. There are
also many templates for organizers: Invite Virtual Meeting Organizer, Update Virtual Meeting
Organizer, and Cancel Virtual Meeting Organizer. Customize your email templates by adding
content, company logo, changing the color scheme, or adjusting the formatting until it
resembles your brand. See Email Templates for more information.
To customize a template, use the available variables ($attendee or $location) belonging to
the template to create a customized email message. See the example below:
$organizer has invited you to join the following meeting:
$name, on $date, at $time.
$agenda:
Conference Access Code: $accesscode
Ways to join the meeting:
SIP Video/Audio sip:$accesscode@sip.deltapath.com
Audio Dial-in (PSTN): Tel:+14085820444,,$accesscode#
Note: The virtual meeting room opens 5 minutes before the scheduled meeting time.
Your email templates must include a conference access code and at least one method(s) of
contact such as PSTN and SIP Video/Audio contact information. In the example above, there
are different ways to join the meeting: SIP Video/Audio sip: $accesscode@sip.deltapath.com
and Audio Dial-in (PSTN): Tel:+1 408 582 0444, $accesscode#. Also, you must let meeting
participants know that conference rooms open 5 minutes before the scheduled meeting. They
will not have access if they try to dial into the conference earlier.
The SIP Video/Audio contact information, must be typed as followed:
• sip:$accesscode@ an FQDN (Fully Qualified Domain Name) or an IP address.

-- 513 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 513
• Meeting participants receive a URL such as 1234567890@sip.deltapath.com which they
can directly enter into the SIP endpoint or they can click the link
sip:1234567890@sip.deltapath.com in their email to automatically join the
conference.
Note: Users who want to have a SIP Video/Audio experience can download Polycom
RealPresence Mobile. See Mobility App for more information. By default, Android and iOS
systems do not support SIP Video/Audio calls. As a result, third-party software is needed.
This is also another option for mobile devices that cannot access the Audio Dial-in (PSTN)
link.
Audio Dial-in (PSTN) must be typed exactly as it is illustrated below:
• Tel: phone number,,$accesscode# such as Tel:+140885820444,,$accesscode#. Notice
there are no spaces and there are two commas between the last digit of the telephone
number and dollar sign. When this information is entered correctly, meeting
participants only need to press the link to automatically join the conference,
eliminating the need to memorize and enter lengthy conference PINs.
Skype for Business Video Gateway
If you have a Skype for Business Video Gateway, virtual meeting participants will have even
more ways to join a meeting including the use of Skype for Business, Skype for Business
Consumer, and WebRTC (Using a Browser).
With SFB Video Gateway, the customized template will look more like this:
$organizer has invited you to join the following meeting:
$name, on $date, at $time.
$agenda:
Conference Access Code: $accesscode
Ways to join the meeting:
SIP Video/Audio sip:$accesscode@sip.deltapath.com
Audio Dial-in (PSTN): Tel:+14085820444,,$accesscode#
Consumer Skype: skype:$accesscode@sip.deltapath.com?call
Skype for Business Client: im:sip:$accesscode@sip.deltapath.com
WebRTC:
http://DELTAPATH.ADDRESS/RESTful/index.php/v1/get/virtualmeeting/accesscode/$accesscode/vide
o/
Note: The virtual meeting room opens 5 minutes before the scheduled meeting time.

58.2 Scheduling a Virtual Meeting
This section is placed in the Administrator guide so admin can assist users in setting up virtual
meetings. Users can create virtual meetings by logging into their user portal from their
browser or switchboard on desktop.
Meetings are based on the user’s local PC time.
1. Click
2. Type Virtual Meeting in the textbox.
3. Click Virtual Meeting.
4. Click Add Virtual Meeting. The Virtual Meeting dialog box opens.

59.3 Conference Panel
Conference hosts can do several things from the Conference Panel. Hosts can click to join
their own conference, send a conference invitation to a single recipient or conference
invitations to a recipient group. The recipient’s phone rings after an invitation is sent. Once
the invited guests answer the call, they are automatically connected to the conference. To
enjoy the Conference Panel, it requires you to add permissions in the ACL Group. For more
information, see Access Control List (ACL) Group.
To join a call from the conference panel, host must do the following:
1. Click
2. Type Conference Panel.
3. Click Conference Panel.
4. Click View Conference next to your conference room.
5. Click Join.

conference by doing the following from the Conference Panel.
1. Participant Options: Set to Single Recipient or Call Recipient Group.
2. Enter a participant’s phone number or the numbers of all the group participants.
3. Click Send invitation.
A dialog box opens letting the host know the meeting invitation has been sent.
Conference hosts can change their conference room password in the Conference Panel, mute
callers, and even lock the conference to prevent late participants from interrupting the
conference.
Before a conference host can select a call recipient group, recipient groups must be created.
Participant groups are very useful if a department or business holds regular meetings with the
same people. Users can create participant groups by following the steps below.

59.4 Global Audio Conference Bridge
Conference Bridge provides a platform for you and other participants to call into the same
bridge and hold a conference. It is important to keep your conversations confidential. As a
result, it is important that the conference PIN for each room be changed prior to a new
conference. Before creating a conference room, define the main extension/number for the
conference. This number acts as a main hall of all conference rooms. All conference rooms
share this main conference extension. The conference room number and PIN are requested
when meeting participants call the main extension/number.

60 Team Sharing Features
60.1 Shared Group Appearance
Some organizations may need to create a shared group line for departments that have a high
volume of call transfers. Here are some of the features and benefits of the Shared Group
Appearance feature:
▪ All the phones in a shared group do not ring with an incoming call as this would be
disruptive for everyone in the group.
▪ All lines do not share a phone number across multiple devices.
▪ No need for call transfers. Calls can be placed on hold and then picked up from
another device by another person with the press of a button since everyone in the
group sees all the callers on hold.
▪ No complex phone features and buttons need to be remembered to use Shared Group
Appearance.
▪ Shared Group Appearance can also improve productivity as the person that is
attempting to transfer the call does not have to wait for the other individual to pick
up the call.
Things to note with Shared Group Appearance
• Shared Group Appearance is only available if users are using Polycom phones.
• When this feature is enabled, call forwarding is disabled as a result of Polycom
firmware limitation.
• When this feature is enabled, members in the same group cannot make outgoing calls
until the incoming call is answered.
• If the Shared Group appearance feature is enabled, members in the same group
cannot make outgoing calls until the incoming call is answered.
Create a Shared Group
1. Click
2. Type Shared Group Appearance in the textbox.
3. Click Shared Group Appearance.
4. Click Add Shared Group Appearance.

-- 524 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 524
5. Group Name: Enter a name. Example: Sales Team or Technical Support Team
6. Customer: Click the dropdown and then Click the customer the group belongs to.
7. Description: This is an optional field that can be used to add additional information
8. Group Members: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click the extensions or phone
devices of the people that will make up the group. Repeat this step until all group
members have been added.
9. Click OK.
Once Shared Group Appearance is set up, reboot the phones of everyone in the group. See
Equipment for more information on rebooting.
60.2 Barge in
By default, the Barge in feature is not enabled on Shared Group Appearance. It can be
enabled under User Settings, which allows a user to barge into another person’s line.
The Barge in feature offers flexibility. It can be enabled as needed and then disabled. Based
on the screenshot above, the barge-in feature may be enabled permanently for all group
members at extension 2oo, 2531, 300, and 7009 so members can barge into each other’s calls
whenever the situation arises. For example, a colleague on a call may instant message (IM)
another colleague for assistance. This individual can simply barge into the call.
60.3 Call/Pickup Group
Call/Pickup group is a popular feature that allows a selective number of people to pick up
their coworkers’ calls. The feature is accessed by pressing a button called Pickup, which
redirects a ringing extension to the phone of the individual that pressed the Pickup button.
This means that if a colleague’s phone is ringing, others in the Call/Pickup Group can answer

-- 525 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 525
the call from their own phones instead of walking over to their coworker’s desk to answer the
call.
The major benefit of the Call/Pickup Group is founded on customer service and customer
satisfaction. With this feature, organizations increase the probability of customer calls being
answered in a reasonable timeframe instead of going to voicemail. This leads to faster
resolutions, increased customer service and increased customer satisfaction.
A Call Group is comprised of the members with the same specialization who work in a team
environment to provide optimal customer service to callers, improve efficiency as well as
productivity. Pickup Group allows users to pick up incoming calls within their own group. For
example, you may create a Call/Pickup Group that only handles sales inquiries and another
Call/Pickup Group that handles Life Insurance inquiries. It is recommended that you separate
Call/Pickup Groups by specialization which ensures calls do not get routed to the wrong
people and ensures customer experience is never negatively impacted.
Create a Call/Pickup Group
To create a Call/Pickup Group you need to do the following:
▪ Identify the Call Group members and Pickup Group Members
▪ Create a pickup number
▪ Create a speed dial
▪ Reboot phones
Activate Call Pickup Feature
Before you define the call group members and the pickup group members, you will need to
decide whether you want to use the system’s default pickup number (*8) or create your own
pickup number. This is the number Pickup Group Members dial to pick up calls for Call Group
Members. If you want to create your own pickup number, follow the steps below.
1. Click
Type Configuration.
2. Click General Settings. The General Settings dialog opens.
3. Click the Feature tab.
4. Feature Code: Call Pickup: Enter a call pickup number. By default, it is set to *8.
5. Click OK.
Setup Call Group Members and Pickup Group Members
1. Click
2. Type Call / Pickup Group.
3. Click Call / Pickup Group. The Call / Pickup Settings dialog box opens.

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential

Directed Call Pickup
A call pickup group member can answer specific extensions when the situation presents itself.
For example, Eric is a pickup group member for Tom (extension 3200) when Tom is away from
his desk. If Tom’s phone happens to ring, Eric can answer the call on behalf of Tom by dialing
the call pickup number (*8) followed by the group member’s extension number (3200), e.g.,
*83200 to pick up the ringing phone of 3200 right from Eric’s desk.

that can be added to the Call interface for any user is 3. To allow users access to Direct
Pickup on Deltapath Mobile, configure the ACL Group as follows:

Default Pickup Group
Remember that Call/Pickup is set up so the first caller in the call queue, which is also the
person waiting the longest in the queue, will be served first. When you assign a Default
Pickup Group to a pickup group member, the pickup user will be able to override the rule
where the caller waiting the longest must be served first.
Example: There are two Call/Pickup Groups.
Pickup Group1 has the following call group members: 112, 113, 114
Pickup Group 2 has the following call group members: 222, 223, 224
Pickup group member 111 belongs to Pick up Group1 and Pickup Group2 and can therefore
pickup calls for extension 112, 113, 114, 222, 223, and 224. Pickup group member 111 also
has Pickup Group2 set as a Default Pickup Group.
Extension 112 starts ringing at 19:00:00 and extension 222 starts ringing at 19:00:10. Both
phones are currently ringing.

-- 528 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 528
When pickup group member 111 dials the pickup number (*8 or the number you defined), he
will be able to pick up extension 222 although extension 222 started to ring after extension
112. This is because pickup group member 111 has Pickup Group2 assigned as a Default Pickup
Group. If there was no Default Pickup Group defined for 111, then 111 would only be able to
pick up extension 112 because this call came in first.

that the user belongs to from the list.

9. Click OK.
By Pickup Group

61 Hunt Group
Some organizations use an approach called the Hunt Group to distribute phone calls from a
single telephone number to a group of designated people at different extensions in an effort
to assist customer needs. To setup a Hunt Group, follow the steps below:
1. Click
2. Type Hunt Group in the textbox.
3. Click Hunt Group.
4. Click Add.
5. Hunt Group Name: Enter the name of the Hunt Group. Ensure the name is not
oversimplified. For example, if your company has more than one customer service

-- 530 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 530
department, it would be best to include the name of the department when naming the
Hunt Group.
Note: The name can contain upper and lowercase letters (A-Z, a-z) and numbers (0-9)
6. Description: This is an optional field. Use it to add additional details about the Hunt
Group.
7. Customer: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click a customer.
8. Hunt Mode: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click one of the following modes.
9. Ring Timeout: Indicate the number of seconds the system will attempt to ring a number
before moving to the next call recipient member in the Hunt Group.
Note: If you select a mode that skips busy members, then the system will skip those
members based on the Ring timeout setting.
Group Member: Click the dropdown arrow and then click the names of all the group
members. Based on the call volume, these members may do nothing but answer the
phones.
Note: Members are identified by their extension number in the Hunt Group.
61.1 Hunt Modes
Choose how you would like to share the distribution of incoming calls among group members.
Top Down:
When Top Down is selected, Hunt Group members’ phones ring one at a time according to
where their names appear in the Group member list. Based on the screenshot below, Brian’s
phone will ring for 10 seconds. If Brian does not answer the call after 10 seconds, the system
will then ring Sherry for 10 seconds. The system will continue to move down the list of group
members until one of the group members answers or until it has tried all group members.

-- 531 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 531
In the event the system has attempted to call all group members, but no one has answered
the call, the system will follow the steps indicated under Next Step under Number Status.
Circular:
The Circular ring strategy is like the Top-Down ring strategy. However, after the system rings
the final group member in the list, which is Jane Smith in the screenshot above, the system
will circle back to the first member in the list and start the process over again. You can
control the number of times the system repeats the loop by doing the following.
Circular Count: Use the up or down arrow to determine the number of times the system will
loop through the Group Member list.
Loop until caller Hang-up: Click the checkbox instead of entering a number in Circular
Count. If the checkbox is selected, the system loops through the Group Members list until the
call is answered or until the caller terminates the call by hanging up.

-- 532 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 532
Broadcast:
All group member phones ring for 10 seconds when there is an incoming call. Based on the
screenshot below, when a call comes in, four extensions ring simultaneously --- Brian, Sherry,
Betty, and Jane.
Note: If you have many members in your Hunt Group, this ring strategy can be very
disruptive.
Round Robin:
An incoming call is sent evenly across different agents. In other words, it sends calls down a
predetermined list of agents. For example, agents X, Y, and Z are currently available to
answer incoming calls. The first call that comes in goes to agent X because the agent is first
in line. The next call goes to agent Y, and then Z.
Skip Busy Member:
If you select a mode that skips busy members, then the system will skip those members based
on the Ring timeout setting.

-- 533 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 533

62 Executive/Assistant Features
62.1 Shared Line Appearance (SLA)
Shared Line Appearance is used in many organizations around the world. Shared Line
Appearance involves creating a simple key (button) function on the phone that is shared with
another extension.
Overview:
▪ If you share a line with someone, you share a phone number/extension
▪ Both individuals with a shared line can answer a call on the shared line.
▪ When one person places a call on hold on a shared line, anyone sharing the line can
resume the call.
▪ If the barge-in feature is enabled, the phone with a shared line can barge into an in
progress call on the shared line.
▪ Share voicemail to others if you choose
This feature is common in a CEO – Executive Assistant situation where the assistant’s phone
has a button shared with the CEO’s phone. If the CEO’s extension is 200 and the assistant’s
phone is 7009 then you can setup a shared line by doing the following:
▪ Modify the settings of 200 to enable shared line appearance
Create an Extra phone set account for 200 and map it to the phone of 7009
▪ Allow or deny the barge-in feature. If the barge-in feature is allowed, then 7009, the
Assistant, can barge-in on 200, the current in progress call on the CEO’s phone.
▪ Reboot the phones
Things to note with Shared Line Appearance
▪ Shared Line Appearance is only available if users are using Polycom phones.
▪ When this feature is enabled, call forwarding is disabled because of Polycom
firmware limitation. To forward your extension, you must use the switchboard. For
more information on how to setup call forwarding from the switchboard, see User: My
Schedule.
▪ Note: Call forwarding can still work if the number status is changed.
▪ When this feature is enabled, members in the same group cannot make outgoing calls
until the incoming call is answered.
▪ If the Call Pickup feature is enabled, members in the same group cannot make
outgoing calls until the incoming call is answered.

Barge in Feature
The barge in button can be used to talk to someone already on a call or used to add another
participant to a call that is in progress.
A scenario is used to help you understand how the barge in feature is used. Peter, the CEO, is
on a call with Edwin in accounting. Peter asks his assistant to contact Hong and have him join
the call. Follow the steps below to see how Hong joins Peter’s and Edwin’s call.
1. Assistant calls Hong from her own line, 1835 on a Polycom VVX 1500.
2. Once Hong answers the phone, he is placed on hold by the Assistant.
3. Assistant presses the Executive’s line that connects the assistant to the CEO and lets him
know Hong is on the phone.
4. To connect Hong to the existing call, Press the Barge In button on the phone.
5. Press the More button
6. Press Join. All four people -- the assistant, Jill, Edwin, and Hong - are now in a
conference together.
7. The assistant hangs up leaving the others in the conference.

extension, the intercom button will populate correctly. If, however, line 200 appears

63 Paging Group
Paging Group is also known as Call Paging or Public Announcement. In this section, you learn
how to create a paging group and associate a phone number to the group. When you dial the
Paging group’s number, your phone is transformed into a speaker ready to broadcast a one-
way voice announcement. Your announcement is broadcasted to all the phones you added to
the paging group.
The use of a phone to make announcements is very popular and useful. A few of the benefits
offered by telephone paging are:
• Emergency alert – alert others immediately when an emergency arises
• Broadcast announcements – gets information out fast
• Locating people – improves customer service and productivity
Principals and students use a phone to broadcast morning announcements to the entire
school. Department stores regularly use their phone to notify other personnel that they need
assistance such as more cashiers.
Create a Paging Group:
1. Click
2. Type Paging Group. The Paging Group dialog box opens.
3. Click Add Paging Group.
4. Name: Enter a name for the Paging Group. Example: Tech Support Team or Third Floor.
5. Description: This is an optional field where additional information can be added.
6. Customer: Click the dropdown arrow and then click a customer.
7. Paging Group Members: Click the dropdown arrow and then Click to add a member to the
paging group. Repeat this step until all members have been added.
The number of people that can be added to a paging group is dictated by the
system you are running. The U32 model can accommodate up to 32 people. The
U256 can have up to 256 people, and a U512 can have up to 512 people.

65 Corporate directory
65.1 Microsoft Active Directory Integration (AD)/LDAP Integration
Deltapath UC can sync with an AD/LDAP server so administrators can create and delete users
on Deltapath’s server or the AD/LDAP server. If you want new users to have AD /LDAP
credentials, create them on the AD/LDAP server. In addition, when LDAP integration and
account mapping are completed, Deltapath Switchboard and other Deltapath applications
can accept AD/LDAP user credentials. Credentials are passed to the Microsoft AD server or
LDAP server for verification. As a result, users will not need to remember multiple user
credentials for different applications.
Sync Threshold
The Sync Threshold is part of the LDAP Integration Settings that is presented as a percentage.
It is a key feature that needs to be understood. It is first and foremost a safety feature
introduced to improve data synchronization success by preventing data loss, or potentially
incorrect or undesired data to sync.
The next section gives you examples to help you consider what the percentage of your sync
threshold should be.
Example 1: The data you are getting from the LDAP server needs to be x% of your existing
user account. Currently, you have 100 existing customers on Deltapath UC and you set your
threshold to 90%. If the list you get from the LDAP server shows 95 users, it appears the copy
of the user data is accurate because it exceeds the threshold. As a result, the system will
trust the copy and synchronize the data.

-- 549 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 549
Example 2: If your company lays off 50% of its workforce and your sync threshold is set to
95%, synchronization will not occur as the data will not be trusted. In this example data
integrity appears to be compromised. The Sync Threshold in this example worked perfectly.
However, this is a reminder to revisit the Sync threshold percent from time to time,
especially when your organization is seeing high rates of turnover or layoffs.
Example 3: Your Deltapath UC system is new. As a result, you want everything to synchronize.
Data integrity is not an issue at the current time. As a result, the Sync Threshold can be set
low.
Best Practice is to set the sync threshold to a high number such as 80 percent. The exception
is situations like Example 3.
LDAP Integration Settings
LDAP integration has two parts: one is login related and the other is related to
synchronization of users.
1. Click
2. Type LDAP.
3. Click LDAP Integration Settings.
4. Address: Enter the AD/LDAP address.
5. Port: Select 389 if it is not preselected.
Note: You can force a secure connection between the AD server and Deltapath server by
using port 636.
6. Base DN: Enter the domain name. Example: deltapath.com
Note: This is an optional setting based on your AD/LDAP. If your users are accustomed to
signing in using just their username, but your AD/LDAP requires username@domain then
placing the domain in the Base DN textbox allows users to continue using only their
username. It is also important to note that if the domain is always appended, users who
type username@domain will have an issue signing in as the @ sign and domain will appear
twice based on your configuration. Example: sherry.see@deltapath.com@deltapath.com
Login Mapping Settings:
7. Status:
Select Enabled to allow user to use LDAP account login to login to Deltapath UC.
Select Disabled if user will not be able to use LDAP account login in Deltapath UC.
8. Login Mapping Suffix: Enter the login suffix to allow users to login without entering the
trailing parts of the username. This is just like Base DN.
Synchronization Settings:
9. Status:
Select Enabled to allow synchronization.
Select Disabled to allow synchronization.

Phonebook Integration
Deltapath UC platform supports integration with LDAP based corporate directories. Once
linked to a corporate directory, the records (based on the filters and attributes you
configured) will display in users’ switchboards, Polycom IP phones under the
SERVICES/APPLICATIONS button, as well as the administrative screen. To ensure Deltapath
UC platform integrates successfully with LDAP based corporate directories, you can use the
Test Connection button located in the Corporate Phonebook Settings dialog box, to verify
successful connection to LDAP.
SUPPORTED V3 LDAP SERVERS
The following LDAP servers have been tested with Deltapath UC’s PBX Corporate Directory
feature:
Microsoft Active Directory 2003
OPEN LDAP 2.4.12
Microsoft ADAM (Active Directory Application Mode) 1.0 SP1
65.1.4.1 Best Practices when using Corporate Directory
When the corporate directory features are enabled, the following practices are
recommended:
1. You may experience high server CPU utilization on your LDAP server if you sustain 20+
LDAP queries per second. For large deployments, we recommend to load balance
Deltapath UC platform LDAP queries over multiple LDAP servers.
2. Anonymous bindings are not supported. The Deltapath UC platform must be configured
with an active userId/password in order to bind.
3. Referrals are not supported.
65.1.4.2 Configuring the Corporate Directory
Example of an LDAP Structure:
The figure below shows an illustration of a simple directory structure that is used for setup.
Every object in a directory has a distinguished named (dn). The distinguished name is
important as it provides the exact location for an object within a directory. When you go
deeper into a directory, you’ll notice that an object inherits part of the dn of its parent
object. In the example, a phone user’s account is created that is used by the Deltapath UC
platform PBX to browse the directory. In addition, specify the baseDN, which points to a
specific place in the directory where the search should start. Pointing the baseDN to the root
(in our example: dc=Polycomqa, dc=local) and setting the directory scope to sub so it
searches through sub directories is sufficient for small directories. However, for larger
directories, minimize the scope in which searches have to browse to improve search
performance. As a result, point the baseDN to the part of the directory where searches are
more relevant.

-- 552 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 552
Figure 28: Corporate Directory Structure
Microsoft Active Directory: By default, the Microsoft Active Directory supports the LDAP
server service. Use the Active
Directory Users and Computers utility to populate your directory.

Active Directory Folder: polycomqa.local/Users
The LDAP distinguished name would be:
cn=ldap user, ou=users, dc=polycomqa, dc=local
Step 2: Note the IP or Hostname and TCP port of your Microsoft Active Directory.
By default, Microsoft Active Directory uses TCP port 389 for LDAP communication. Refer to
your Microsoft documentation if you want to change the default port.
Step 3: Determine the base search you want to use for your searches.
Based on the Active Directory Users and Computer dialog box below, search from the root of
the directory, polycomqa.local. In the LDAP User Properties dialog box above, the baseDN
would be DC=polycomqa, DC=local.

-- 555 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 555
Step 4: Determine the filter prefix you would like to use.
By default, (objectclass=*) will show all objects in the search base. Examples of other type of
filters:
(sn=*) Only display records with a surname attribute
(objectclass=person) Only display records that has an object class person which
exclude network devices/computers.
Using the field “objectcategory” in the filter prefix (dir.corp.filterPrefix) can be more
efficient for the server than using “objectclass” (the default value), since the former is
indexed on the Active Directory server by default, whereas the latter is not indexed by
default.
Step 5: Determine if you will require recursive searching
The default is to perform a recursive search in the baseDN: dir.corp.scope=sub. Since we are
pointing to the root folder, we would like the Deltapath UC platform PBX to look at sub
folders within the root of the active directory. To do a flat search, set dir.corp.scope=one.
Step 6: Determine what attributes you would need to show on the phone sn, givenName and
telephone number are common attributes to set up. However, you can include many
attributes from your directory. These attributes are useful when you are trying to filter your
search in advance find. For example, there is an attribute called department that you can use
to filter a list of users by department. You can define a maximum of eight attributes.
Attributes are case sensitive, so ensure that they match your schema. For more information
on attributes, refer to http://support.microsoft.com/kb/555638

66 E911
66.1 E911 Emergency Call Handling
The E911 feature provides an effective way for emergency response personnel to handle
emergency calls such as 911. The objectives of this feature are:
• To engage Security Personnel when a user dials 911 to the 911 Dispatch Center.
o To provide location information for the user dialing 911. The information needs to
be accurate about the floor and building number.
o To allow the Security Personnel, 911 caller, and 911 hotline to be in a conference
call.
• To record the entire conversation from start to end.

-- 557 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 557
• To access the E911 page, select the E911 and have a small window open.
To achieve an E911 call, a security team must first be defined. A security team is a group of
phones that will receive calls from a blue light phone as well as anyone in the system that
dialed E911 Number (known as the emergency number defined by the System Administrator).
A blue light phone is a device that has a single button for calling and directly connects to a
hotline.
To register a blue light phone, you need to assign it with an extension number and input its Mac
address on the user page. After inputting the Mac address, you should see the blue light phone
model. Once you are done selecting the phone model, you will be provided with a field on
where to send the call to.
Once the blue light phone is configured in the system, obtain the IP address of the device, and
access it via the web interface and add in the provisioning server IP address.
Security Team:
You must define a team name and select the phone numbers of the security team members so
that those phones will ring at the same time and the security member will be informed.
Send call to: Security Team: once this is selected, the field to the right will show the
security team names.
Number: once this is selected, you will be provided with a blank field to
enter the emergency number that the blue light phone should connect to.
The number should be the E911 hotline number in most cases unless
special number is to be called.

67 Logical Partitioning for Compliance in Specific Countries
In certain countries, such as India, regulatory constraints forbid the interconnection of VoIP
and PSTN call routing. To conform with the regulations in these countries, logical partitioning
is used to prevent calls from bypassing applicable toll charges.
With logical partitioning, the call restriction setting cannot be set to No. Instead, the
call restriction setting should be set to either Yes, PSTN, or VOIP.
Parameter: Description:
Team
name
Team name to quickly identify the group
Description Information to describe the group
Member Select the users that are to be a part of the security team
Parameter: Description:
IP Address Input the IP address or the network range for all the facilities that will be using
the E911 feature.
CIDR If CIDR is not checked, the mapping would be a single IP address only. If
selected, a box for subnet will be provided so that a network range would be
defined. See the CIDR table for more information.
Location Information of where the caller is calling from, determine by matching the
subnet and IP address of the caller’s phone.

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 559
The diagram below illustrates the separation of PSTN and VOIP calls as a result of logical
partitioning.
Figure 29: Logical Partitioning - Separation of PSTN and VOIP Calls
In the illustration above, there is no possible way to establish connectivity between systems
through the phones. Line 1 and Line 2 work independently of each other and therefore,
cannot join calls.
67.1 Special Notes / Requirements for Logical Partitioning
• A PBX that requires logical partitioning must set CALL TYPE of all the SIP Trunks and
gateway to either VoIP or PSTN, depending on whether the peer is associated as a VoIP
or PSTN receiver. (Gateway is typically PSTN while SIP Trunks are VoIP)
• All users CALL RESTRICTION setting: Select YES, PSTN or VoIP, depending on its usage.
• For incoming calls, when party A is already in a VoIP call with party B, an incoming call
from PSTN by party C will always be routed to the voice mailbox of party A.
• When users in the same PBX call each other, the call is treated as local call. As a result,
an incoming call from either VoIP or PSTN will always be received.

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0

67.2 Logical Partitioning Call Flow
The following diagrams illustrate call flows for Logical Partitioning between VoIP and
PSTN calls.
In the figure below, logical partitioning prevents an incoming call from PSTN because
the user is already engaged in a VoIP call. There cannot be a mix of PSTN + VoIP.
VOIP Call
PSTN
Mobile Call
Hong Kong	India
Figure 30: Logical Partitioning - VOIP Call in Progress and Incoming PSTN Call
A user cannot make outgoing calls to PSTN if engaged in a VoIP call with another user in a
different site location. In addition, a user cannot make a call transfer or a conference to a
PSTN line if the user is engaged in a VoIP call.
When a user is engaged in a VoIP call to another site, the user can receive calls and transfer
calls as well as conduct a conference call between the VoIP calls. This is also true for PSTN
calls if the call types are the same.

67.3 Configuration Guide for Logical Partitioning
A prerequisite of enabling logical partitioning is to configure the SIP Trunk, gateway and user
profile as these 2 configurations are critical for logical partitioning to function.
Setup SIP Trunk
To setup the SIP Trunk for logical partitioning,
1. Click
2. Enter SIP Trunk in the textbox.
3. Click SIP Trunk.
4. Click the pencil to modify an existing SIP Trunk or add a new SIP Trunk.
5. Scroll down to Call Restriction and select the appropriate option.
Setup Gateway
1. Click
2. Type Gateway in textbox.
3. Click Gateway.
4. Select the gateway you are using.
Once a gateway is selected, click modify to edit a gateway’s configuration or Click Add
AudioCodes to add a new gateway. Complete the CALL RESTRICTION option.
Setup User Profile for Logical Partitioning
1. Click
2. Type User in the textbox.
3. Click User.
4. Click User Profile. Click to modify an existing profile or Add Profile to add a new
profile.
5. Locate the Call Restriction textbox under Profile Settings.
6. Click the dropdown arrow and then Click
• YES: Enable call control of both PSTN and VOIP. System can identify the type of call
that is currently running and will not accept a second call if the current call does not
match with the current one.
• NO: Call restriction is not enabled. The trunk will be able to mix calls (VoIP + PSTN).
• PSTN: The trunk only supports PSTN. Users cannot receive or make a call tagged as VoIP.
• VOIP: The trunk only supports VoIP. Users cannot receive or make a call tagged as PSTN.

70 Maintenance and Support
70.1 System Recovery
System recovery is a tool in Deltapath UC platform used to run a scheduled backup of the
system. Will you backup internally or externally (FTP)? What do you want to back up and
when? Serious consideration should be given to your backup plan.

Table 88: System Recovery
In the event of data loss, you can rollback by going to System Recovery and then recovery
Backup Image. Choose the backup and restore. If you store backups externally, you can
upload the file first and then follow the same steps.
70.2 System Status
System Status helps you determine server health, gateway traffic, and user status and
provides a high level of accuracy to help you manage your system effectively. If in doubt
about the errors being reported from Deltapath UC platform, open a ticket with Deltapath
Support Team via https://support.deltapath.com
Active Call List
Displays the current active calls in the PBX, PSTN calls or/and VOIP calls. It provides the call
start time, duration, caller number, codec in use for the call, destination number, and
destination codec. The call history of this list is captured in the Call Details Record (CDR).
Active Peer List
Provides the peer status defined in the PBX. It contains a summary of the name, username,
IP address, port number and Keepalive status.
Device Call Forward List
Users can configure a Cisco phone to forward calls to another number. The Device Call
Forward List captures all Cisco endpoints that have call forwarding turned on. This list helps

-- 581 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 581
administrators quickly troubleshoot phone issues. For example, administrators can confirm
whether users have call forwarding turned on.
Gateway Call List
Provides information of active calls passing through the selected gateway. It shows a
summary of the source and destination number, duration, port used and channel used for the
current call. It also gives you the option to disconnect a selected call by clicking the
Disconnect icon found on the far-right side of the active call.

72 Appendix 2
These settings will dictate whether RTP traffic flows through the Deltapath appliance or if it
is peer to peer. All devices (with a few exceptions) will follow the settings listed below.
72.1 General Settings
On the Network tab, administrators may choose to “Force RTP Packets Through Deltapath®
Server For All Users.”
72.2 SIP Trunk Settings
A similar option is available under Trunk Settings.
72.3 User Profile
User profile settings will also influence RTP flows:
• For devices set to Outside Network, RTP will route through Deltapath®.
• For devices set to Inside Network, RTP will route directly between the endpoints.*
*Subject to change based on the other settings listed above as well as the settings
defined by the opposite peer.

-- 589 of 590 --

Deltapath UC Platform Administrator Guide, Release 5.3 v1.0
Copyright © 2024 by Deltapath, Inc. All Rights Reserved. Confidential
For Existing Deltapath Customers Only | 589
72.4 Call Recording
If call recording is enabled, all RTP traffic will be routed through Deltapath® in order to
capture data.
72.5 Exceptions
The following equipment will always be forced to disable direct media (RTP traffic will always
flow through Deltapath Deltapath®) regardless of settings:
• All Quintum analog lines
• All AudioCodes FXS analog lines
• Equipment configured as “Own CPE” as well as others with T.38 enabled
• Cisco-CP-DX
• Mobicents
• Linphone
• X-Lite
• eyeBeam
